31. Recurrent-depth: a false "seed noise" verdict, two methodology bugs found, and a literature-driven three-phase fix

2026-07-06 (afternoon)

The seed-1 confirmation from §30 finished: val_ppl 83.30 — worse than control (81.20), and worse than seed-0's 75.91. On its face, a third instance of the exact failure pattern that closed MoE and Engram: a striking single-seed win that inverts on reseed. Followed the established discipline and banked recurrent-depth as inconclusive-to-negative, matching those two precedents.

But Glenn pushed back: this architecture is a real sales-pitch asset if it works, worth a deeper pass before writing it off. Asked for research into whether there's a fix, new logic, or different approach — not just "try another seed."

Literature review surfaced the field's standard recipe — and we were missing a piece of it

Current 2025-26 looped-transformer work converges on a few facts directly relevant to our situation. Ouro (ByteDance Seed, arXiv:2510.25741) is the flagship proof this class of architecture works at all: a 1.4B looped model matches 4-8B dense models, trained on 7.7T tokens with losses computed at every loop exit, not just the final one. LoopFormer and other 2026 looped-model papers use the same dense per-loop supervision as standard. Our recurrent-depth rewrite (§29) never did this — only the final pass ever received a training loss, so pass-0 representations were only ever refined indirectly through pass-1's gradient, never trained as a decodable output in their own right. This plausibly explains the odd convergence shape both depth-2 screens showed: still falling steeply at budget end while every sibling config at this recipe plateaus by then.

Two cautions from the same literature tempered how aggressively to apply this: a "readout blind spot" finding (dense supervision without care can teach the model to shortcut straight to the final readout instead of doing real work in earlier passes) and a hidden-state norm growth finding (RMSNorm can hide runaway scale from the loss under looped training). Both argue for a conservative first bet, not the field's typical full weight-1.0 per exit.

Two real bugs found in the comparison methodology itself

Digging into exactly what "control = 81.20" meant surfaced something more fundamental than a training-recipe gap:

  • Unmatched token budgets. The 81.20 control was trained on 100M tokens. Both depth-2 results being judged against it were trained on 200M — twice the data of what they were compared to. No depth-1 @ 200M control had ever been run. Every recurrent-depth screen this project has done compared apples to oranges on this axis.
  • Eval sampling noise. evaluate() drew 20 random validation batches per call, with no fixed seed — a different sample of windows every single measurement, both across evals within one run and across different configs being compared.
Re-evaluating the two existing depth-2 checkpoints under a fixed, deterministic 100-iteration eval collapsed their gap from 75.91/83.30 (7.4 ppl apart, looked like a dramatic sign flip) to 77.35/78.52 (1.2 ppl apart). Most of "seed1 didn't confirm" was measurement noise, not a real quality reversal — the two seeds actually agree closely once measured fairly. This calls into question how much of the MoE and Engram "seed noise" closures (and any other borderline call made before this fix) were partly this same artifact, though those had larger, more consistent gaps and likely still hold.

Three-phase fix, staged to fail cheap

Phase 0 (fix the ruler): a module-level fixed eval seed in train_gpu.py now makes every evaluate() call draw the identical validation windows, for every config, every time — eliminating the sampling-noise confound project-wide going forward (iters also bumped 20→100 for a further variance cut). The actually-missing depth-1 @ 200M control (two seeds) launched on the 5080 to finally give a fair, matched comparison.

Phase 1 (deep supervision): built the missing piece from the literature — each non-final recurrent pass now optionally projects through the shared output head and receives its own weighted cross-entropy term (recurrent_deep_supervision, default weight 0.3 per intermediate pass, deliberately conservative given the readout-blind-spot and norm-growth cautions above). Verified as an exact no-op at depth-1, correct at depth-2, compatible with the Stage-2 router. Staged, gated on Phase 0's verdict.

Phase 2 (train-time memory-carry, the "split-replay at training time" idea) hit a real design flaw before any code was written. The original pitch: carry the Hebbian memory across recurrent passes over the same sequence, so pass 2 re-reads with memory pass 1 already built — the train-time analog of split-replay's proven 2-4x inference-time recall lift. Working through the mechanics: by the end of pass 1, memory holds associations from every position in the sequence including the tail. If pass 2 then predicts next-tokens using that memory, it leaks future-within-the-same-example information into earlier predictions — inflating the loss without teaching anything that survives to genuine autoregressive generation. This differs from how split-replay works at inference, where "build" and "query" read genuinely different content (a context, then questions about it) — no leakage, because you're recalling, not predicting the build content back. Deferred; the corrected, causally-sound version (apply the same idea to MQAR-style synthetic recall training, which already has a real build/query split) is banked for later rather than built alongside two other live variables.
Where this stands. Recurrent-depth is REOPENED, not closed — the "banked as inconclusive" verdict earlier in this same session did not survive scrutiny of its own methodology. Phase 0 (matched control under a fair eval) running on the 5080. Phase 1 (deep supervision) built and staged behind it. Phase 2 deferred with a corrected design in reserve. 500M v2 continues clean on the 5090 throughout (~1.85B/5B tokens).

32. Recurrent-depth (uniform) closes; MoR's actual per-token routing claim was never tested — that's next

Phase 0 finished: the fair, fixed-eval depth-1@200M control came back at 74.55 / 73.35 (seeds 0/1) — beating both plain depth-2 (77.35 / 78.52) and depth-2 + deep supervision (79.49 / 76.94, seed-confirmed). Deep supervision (Phase 1) didn't rescue depth-2; it's statistically flat vs. the un-supervised depth-2 baseline. Uniform recurrent-depth (loop the whole block stack N times, every token, every pass) closes as negative at this 30M/200M-token scale — a clean third instance of the project's "striking single-seed result doesn't survive reseed/fair-control" pattern, alongside MoE and Engram.

Web research (arXiv:2507.10524, the original Mixture-of-Recursions paper) surfaced an important scope gap: everything tested above is uniform looping (Geiping/Huginn-style — every token gets every pass), which is a weaker, cheaper idea than MoR's actual contribution. MoR's headline result (beats a dense baseline at matched FLOPs) comes specifically from the expert-choice router making depth adaptive per token — easy tokens exit after one pass, hard tokens get more. We already built this mechanism (recurrent_router_enabled, RecurrentDepthRouter in adaptive_compute.py, "select AND scale" gated-residual fix already landed) but never ran it inside this session's fair-comparison arc. The paper's own ablations otherwise match what we already have: expert-choice + linear sigmoid router + declining capacity schedule + calibration-BCE aux loss. One design gap worth banking for later: MoR's biggest ablation win was "middle-cycle" parameter sharing (unique first/last layer, only middle layers shared) — a no-op at our depth-2, but relevant if depth ever goes to 3+.

Launched screen_rdepth2_mor_200mtok_30m.yaml (Stage 2, router-gated) on the 5080 as the actual test of MoR's claim. Per the paper, the win to look for isn't necessarily lower val_ppl at fixed depth — it's matched-or-better quality at fewer FLOPs (their own result: lower ppl AND -25% memory AND -19% train time, not ppl alone). keep_rate/depth_hist (already logged) are the diagnostic to watch regardless of the ppl verdict: if routing genuinely splits tokens (not collapsed to all-continue or all-exit), that's a real per-token-adaptive-compute result worth having even if ppl is flat.

33. A scalar-β write-gate collapse was silently killing the delta rule's memory — root-caused and fixed, decisively

2026-07-10

The 90M MoR seed1 re-run (launched to see whether 4-layer scale would resolve the mixed 30M/90M MoR verdict) came back with memory Δ=+0.00 — not a small number, the raw val_ppl and ablated_ppl were IDENTICAL to two decimals across five separate evals. Rather than accept this at face value (or dismiss it as noise), it was run down to ground truth.

Ruled out, in order: a diagnostic artifact (first test used random-noise tokens; a fresh model could plausibly go spike-silent on out-of-distribution input — rerun with real validation text through the exact evaluate() code path, same zero result); a wiring bug (ablate_memory is correctly threaded through every pass of the recurrent-depth+router loop, confirmed by direct code read, and the SAME code path produced a real +13.7 logit difference on seed0); weight collapse in the general sense (W_beta/W_alpha weight NORMS were healthy, comparable to seed0's).

Root cause: a scalar-β write-gate saturation trap. The delta rule computes M ← αM + β·(v − Mk)·kᵀ with β = sigmoid(W_β·spk) — one scalar per position per block. Once W_β·spk drifts below roughly -20, sigmoid bit-zeros, β locks at exactly 0, gradient through β vanishes, and that block's memory M never gets written again for the rest of training. Direct instrumentation of W_beta(spk)'s pre-sigmoid output confirmed all 4 of seed1's blocks had saturated below -20, while seed0 had only 2 of 4 saturated — the other 2 carried the entire model, which is why seed0 "looked fine" (74.66→56.26 val_ppl, healthy-looking memory-Δ +80 to +96) purely by lucky partial collapse. More layers means more independent chances for a block to collapse — this is a per-block stochastic failure, not a per-run one. A literature check found NVIDIA had published the identical diagnosis one week prior: Gated DeltaNet-2 (arXiv:2605.22791, May 2026) calls out the exact same pathology and proposes channel-wise (vector) gates as the fix.

This retroactively reframes the 2026-07-08 MoR result: the 30M screen's ablation-Δ collapse to +2.35 (vs the depth-1 baseline's +15-21) was very likely this same bug, not evidence that MoR is under-scaled below 135M params as the literature-based reasoning concluded at the time. A 2-layer model has only two chances for a block to survive collapse — bad odds.

Two fixes were prototyped as a 30M A/B against the +2.35 baseline: beta_floor: 0.1 (floors β so it can never fully saturate — one config line) and vector_beta: true (per-channel β, matching NVIDIA's actual fix, but forces a sequential memory loop since the chunked/FLA kernel assumes scalar β).

beta_floor: 0.1 won decisively. Final val_ppl 65.87 vs the unfixed baseline's 76.43 (−10.6 ppl — a genuinely better model, not just an unblocked one). Final memory-Δ +94.44 vs +2.35 (~40× recovery). β dead_frac held at 0% across every block, every eval, the entire run — blk1's β sat right at the floor as designed, blk0 saturated high and wrote aggressively, both alive throughout. Energy efficiency also improved, 1.90–2.54× vs transformer (was 1.30× pre-fix). vector_beta was not run to completion — it OOM's at any batch size that fits a reasonable screen budget on 16GB, and beta_floor's margin was already large enough that the comparison wasn't needed.

β telemetry landed alongside the fix (src/model.py::_beta_stats, printed as [beta] blk0: μ=... dead=X% after every [eval] line, flags COLLAPSED at ≥95% dead) — this pathology is now visible live in every training log going forward, not discoverable only via a post-hoc diagnostic script. beta_floor: 0.1 is now the standing default for every delta_rule config; it was baked into all four queued A/B screens (NorMuon control/treatment, Engram v2 control/treatment) so none of them re-inherit the collapse. A 90M seed1 re-validation with the fix (the exact checkpoint/failure-mode that motivated this whole investigation) launched immediately after and is in progress — first eval already shows memory-Δ +72.66 with all 4 blocks at dead_frac 0%, a strong early signal that the fix holds at 4-layer scale.

Open decision, deferred pending the 90M validation result: the in-flight 350M MoR run was built and trained entirely pre-fix (paused by Glenn at step 8000/302M tokens, memory-Δ +45.91 at pause time — unknown how many of its 5 blocks are actually collapsed, since it predates the β telemetry). Whether to restart it with the fix or replan a new 350M incorporating everything learned since it was built (β-fix, possibly NorMuon, updated recipe defaults) is the next call.

34. LoRA/PEFT lands, post-pretrain refine configs rebuilt, 500M v2 plateau diagnosed as convergence not stagnation

2026-07-10

The 90M seed1 β-fix validation (launched at the end of the last session) got its second eval point today: memory-Δ climbed +72.66 → +94.90, val_ppl 104.98 → 79.56, all 4 blocks still dead_frac 0%. Two data points in a row confirms the fix holds at 4-layer scale, not a single-eval blip.

500M v2 fast's eval line had been essentially frozen for ~25 consecutive evals (~750M tokens): val_ppl 15.03–15.07, memory-Δ +30.24–+30.39, spike pinned at 0.069 — all inside noise-floor range of each other. Rather than treat this as something to fix with a late intervention, checked the LR schedule: at ~88% through the cosine decay, Muon LR is already down to ~0.0007 (from the peak 0.005). The plateau is the recipe converging on schedule, not stalling — a late-cosine LR bump, distribution-shifting config change, or restart this close to the end is a real risk of undoing gains for a coin-flip chance at a small one. Decision: let the last ~2 days finish clean, then extract the free wins that are specifically designed for this moment — PMA soup + the two post-pretrain refine phases.

LoRA/PEFT built and CPU-verified end to end. The to-do's priority-1 "new architectural idea" (previously unstarted) is now real: src/lora.py (LoRALinear — zero-init B = exact no-op at injection, frozen base; inject_lora targets the delta-rule W_q/W_k/W_v projections by default and explicitly skips the degenerate 1-row gate matrices so it never touches W_beta/W_gate; adapter-only save/load; merge_lora fold-in for zero-overhead inference), additive wiring into train_sft.py/train_dpo.py behind lora_enabled: false (adapters train via a single AdamW — Muon's Newton-Schulz orthogonalization is the wrong update rule for low-rank factors and would starve AdamW of params entirely under LoRA-only training), and scripts/merge_lora_ckpt.py to bake an adapter into its base for chaining SFT→DPO→demo. Verified against the real SpikingHebbianLM (not just a toy model): injection wraps exactly the expected 6 layers at 2-layer scale, bit-identical output at injection, gradients flow only into adapters, and the full SFT-train→save→merge chain reproduces the adapted output exactly. This is a different kind of "brain mod" than the fast-weight M state (episodic runtime memory) — LoRA is a durable learned skill adapter; the two compose rather than compete.

Audited the post-500M-pretrain pipeline's PMA/MQAR-refine/sparsity-refine assets, all planned back on 2026-07-01/05 before most of the current recipe existed. scripts/pma_average.py needed no changes — correct fp32-averaging, EMA weighting, and non-float-tensor handling, and the live run's disk state is an ideal soup window (8 checkpoints, all inside the flat plateau). configs/mqar_refine_500m.yaml and configs/sparsity_refine_500m.yaml were both stale and would have failed on load: n_neurons: 4352 vs the live model's 4864, no beta_floor, missing every feature landed since (MTP, RMSNorm, embed_scale, z-loss, Markov/confidence heads, depth-scaled-init, FLA kernel, fused CE), and max_tokens budgets sized for an abandoned 2B-token pretrain path. Rewrote both to match the live arch exactly (verified programmatically — every arch-critical key matches the pretrain config, beta_floor: 0.1 baked into both) and revised the sparsity refine's premise: it was written targeting 0.13→0.08 spike, but the live pretrain is already at 0.069, so that refine is now marked optional (softer sparsity_lambda, halved LR to match the smaller remaining gap).

Sketched a design note for hyper-connections (docs/hyperconnections_design.md) rather than build it speculatively: recommends Frac-connections (near-zero memory cost, respects the existing d==d_mem residual gate, exact-identity init for restart safety) over full hyper-connections (rejected on the same 16GB-memory grounds that killed vector_beta), identifies the three mirrored residual-add call sites in model.py that would all need the same connection helper, and sets an explicit decision gate: build and A/B only after NorMuon lands, and require both a 30M A/B and a 90M confirm before it's eligible for the 350M recipe. Consistent with Glenn's call to hold the 350M launch until every enhancement (MoR, NorMuon, Engram v2, possibly Frac-connections) has cleared its own validation — the 350M has no baseline to compare against, so it should launch once, with everything proven in, rather than iterate live across an 11-day run.

Major version: 1.18 (LoRA/PEFT built and CPU-verified against the real model — adapters for delta-rule W_q/W_k/W_v, zero-init no-op at injection, adapter-only save/merge, wired into SFT/DPO behind a default-off flag. Post-pretrain PMA/MQAR-refine/sparsity-refine pipeline audited: PMA script was already correct, but both refine configs were architecturally stale (predating beta_floor and most of the current recipe) and have been rebuilt to match the live 500M v2 fast arch exactly. 500M v2's ~25-eval plateau (val_ppl ~15.05, memory-Δ ~+30.3, spike 0.069) diagnosed as expected late-cosine convergence, not stagnation — left untouched rather than risk a late intervention. 90M seed1 β-fix validation confirmed at a second eval point (+72.66→+94.90, dead_frac still 0% on all 4 blocks). Hyper-connections design note banked, gated behind NorMuon + a 30M+90M confirm before the 350M.)

35. The β-collapse fix holds decisively through a full 90M run — nine eval points, zero collapse

2026-07-10

The 90M seed1 β-fix validation ran to step ~19800 / 324M of its 400M-token budget since the last check-in (which had only two eval points). It now has nine: memory-Δ climbed +72.66 → +94.90 → +123.79 → +161.66 → +177.38 → +206.38 (peak) then settled at +172–178 for the last three — and val_ppl fell monotonically the entire time, 104.98 → 52.77, still dropping at last check. Most importantly: dead_frac read 0% on all 4 blocks at every single one of the nine evals, no exceptions.

This is the confirmation the fix needed before being trusted at scale — not a single early eval that could still be a fluke, a full run's worth of stability on the exact checkpoint/failure-mode (4-layer, seed1) that fully collapsed pre-fix. beta_floor: 0.1 is validated at 4-layer scale as decisively as it was at 30M/2-layer.

Worth being precise about what this does and doesn't unblock. It clears the technical gate from the 2026-07-10 root-cause session ("confirm the fix holds at 4-layer scale before trusting the 350M decision"). It does not by itself green-light the 350M: Glenn's separate, later call was to hold that launch until NorMuon A/B and Engram v2 A/B (and possibly Frac-connections) also clear, so the 350M bakes in every validated enhancement at once rather than launching mid-stream on an 11-day run with no baseline. Those are still queued on GPU time. 500M v2 fast continued clean in the same window, now ~4593M/5000M tokens (~92%), same flat-plateau character as chapter 34 — still converging on schedule.

Follow-up same day: spot-checking the 500M v2 fast log at step 298000 confirmed it is itself a live instance of the pre-fix collapse pathology — demo_phase1b_500m_v2_fast.yaml has no beta_floor set, and 3 of its 5 blocks (blk1, blk3, blk4) read dead=100% COLLAPSED, with only blk0/blk2 alive. Not a new failure, not a surprise given the run predates the fix, and not worth a late restart this close to completion (val_ppl 15.04 and still falling on the 2 surviving blocks). Flagged as a confirmed live example (not just hypothetical) in audit-config.md and neuro_beta_collapse_fix.md so the next config audit catches it automatically; the queued MQAR/sparsity refine configs already bake in beta_floor: 0.1 and should restore the dead blocks during refine.

Major version: 1.19 (90M seed1 β-fix validation ran to completion-in-progress with nine consecutive eval points, all 4 blocks holding dead_frac 0% throughout — memory-Δ peaked +206.38, settled ~+175, val_ppl 104.98→52.77 and still falling. The fix is now decisively confirmed at 4-layer scale, clearing the technical gate for the 350M decision. The 350M launch itself remains deliberately held pending NorMuon + Engram v2 A/Bs per Glenn's kitchen-sink-before-11-days call. 500M v2 fast continues clean, ~4593M/5000M tokens.)

36. Two of three 350M gate items confirmed (NorMuon, Engram v2); the 500M's post-pretrain refine pipeline turns up empty-handed

2026-07-11

The 90M seed1 β-fix run finished cleanly overnight (400.0M tok, 6.55h), and demo_phase1b_500m_v2_fast also finished on its own right around a session restart — 5000.0M tok in 12.57h, freeing the 5090. That kicked off two independent threads: closing out the remaining 350M gate items on the 5080, and running the post-pretrain pipeline (PMA soup → MQAR-refine → sparsity-refine) on the freed 5090.

NorMuon: two bugs, then a real (if modest) win

The NorMuon treatment arm crashed twice before producing a valid result. First, a classic YAML footgun: normuon_eps: 1e-8 parses as the string "1e-8" under PyYAML (scientific notation needs a decimal point — 1.0e-8 — to be recognized as a float), which sailed untyped into the optimizer and crashed on the first step. Fixed the config and added a defensive float() cast in train_gpu.py. Second, once that was cleared, the run diverged instead of crashing — val_ppl climbing 805→1519, spike pinned ~0.46. Root cause: our NorMuon implementation did per-neuron row normalization and then stopped, omitting the global rescale the actual paper (arXiv:2510.05491) applies afterward. Because the Newton-Schulz-orthogonalized update is semi-orthogonal, forcing every row to unit RMS inflates the update's global norm by roughly √max(d_out,d_in) ≈ 45× before the learning rate even applies. Fixed by rescaling the row-normalized update back to the orthogonalized update's own global RMS (verified numerically: RMS ratio 1.000 exactly, per-row spread 0.015→0.000). Both bugs banked in reference_yaml_scinotation_footgun.md and neuro_normuon_renorm_fix.md.

The fixed A/B completed cleanly: NorMuon 85.61 vs Muon control 86.69 — a real quality win, but NorMuon took 28% longer wall-clock (3.32h vs 2.59h), not the "+15%, ~3% overhead" the paper reports at 350M. Quality wins the argument for the kitchen-sink bake; the efficiency claim doesn't hold at this scale and shouldn't be assumed at 350M/500M without re-checking.

Engram v2 survives a seed check — the first enhancement in this project to do so

Ran engram_factual_eval.py on both saved Engram v2 checkpoints (had to patch the eval script first — it was v1-only and would have silently built both models with v2 disabled). Result: v2 beats the no-engram control on every metric — log-prob +0.122, rank −82.5, top-10 +2.5% — a small but consistent, directionally correct signal, unlike v1's ablation-Δ which fired but never moved quality. Then ran the seed1 confirm the project's own history says never to skip: v1 Engram and MoE Phase 1 both had a seed0 "win" that reversed on seed1. This time it held — seed0: v2 43.33 vs control 48.03 (−4.70); seed1: v2 44.30 vs control 48.08 (−3.78).

Engram v2 is the first enhancement in this project's history to survive a seed check. Confirmed, real, goes in the 350M bake.

Frac-connections: built from scratch, verified before spending any GPU time

docs/hyperconnections_design.md's own decision gate said "build after NorMuon lands" — so once NorMuon cleared, built src/fracconn.py: a per-layer learnable mix over 2 fractions of the residual stream, replacing the fixed out + x add at all 3 mirrored call sites in model.py. Both mixing matrices identity-init, so the module is designed to be an exact no-op until trained. Verified this bit-for-bit before any GPU run — isolated module (diff = 0.0 exactly), full-model CPU probe in plain / recurrent-depth / router-ON modes (all diff = 0.0, confirming it composes cleanly with the MoR router loop), and a real forward+backward integration test through the production build_model() path. The control arm (flag off) then completed on GPU with final val_ppl 86.69 — bit-for-bit matching the NorMuon control's own final number on the same recipe/seed, confirming the wiring is genuinely inert at fracconn_enabled: false, not just at init. Treatment arm result pending.

The 500M's post-pretrain refine pipeline: three refinements, all null

While the 5080 worked through the gate items, the 5090 ran the full post-pretrain chain: PMA soup (last 8 ckpts) → MQAR-refine (+50M tok, recall-aug on) → sparsity-refine (+50M tok, higher sparsity penalty). All three completed cleanly. All three did nothing.

PMA soup vs raw-final: val_ppl 15.5534 → 15.5472, a −0.006 change — negligible, nowhere near the 0.2–1.0 the PMA literature reports. MQAR-refine: recall_eval showed no improvement at any N (4 through 64), in single-pass or split-replay mode, and the training log's own memory-Δ sat flat ~30.3–30.7 the whole refine instead of climbing toward the +40 target the config was written to hit. Sparsity-refine: spike rate 0.069→0.069, never moved toward its 0.05 target. Three refinements, one consistent verdict: null.

Chased down whether this was a measurement artifact before trusting it. recall_eval.py's model-builder was missing 7 arch kwargs (RMSNorm, embed-scale, MTP, Markov, confidence, FLA — the same class of bug already fixed in engram_factual_eval.py) and would have silently built the wrong architecture; patched and confirmed zero dropped keys after. Checked the MQAR training augmentation and the eval use byte-identical key/value token ranges (1000–6000 / 8000–14000) — no train/eval distribution mismatch. Confirmed mqar_aug: true genuinely fires a loss term every step, not a silently no-op'd flag. And checked the sample size: at B=64 the standard error at these accuracies is ~6%, well above the 1–2% checkpoint-to-checkpoint spread — the null is real, not noise-starved.

The likely explanation is budget, not uselessness: 50M refine tokens is ~1% of the 5B pretrain, at an auxiliary-loss weight (0.5) the main LM loss simply outweighs. Real mid-training/annealing phases in the literature run 27B+ tokens (arXiv:2510.06826). Separately, there's a published architectural ceiling for DeltaNet-style recall at high N (arXiv:2607.07386, arXiv:2406.06484) that no refine budget will fix — but low-N recall (4–16) still has real headroom. So launched Test 2: mqar_refine_500m_strong.yaml, resumed from the sparsity-refine final, mqar_weight 0.5→3.0 and +200M tokens (4× the original). If recall still doesn't move under recall-dominant training at 4× the budget, the low-N null is architectural too, and MQAR-refine gets dropped from the pipeline for future models rather than re-attempted with an even bigger budget. Full detail: neuro_500m_post_pretrain_refine_null.md.

Gotcha: WSL's memory cap, not the machine, was the instability

Mid-session, background training jobs started dying silently and wsl commands began returning connection errors — looked like environment flakiness until dmesg turned up an actual kernel OOM-kill. .wslconfig had memory=20GB despite the host having 32GB physical — loading multiple ~5GB checkpoints for the PMA/recall tests kept hitting that ceiling. Bumped to 24GB (a full wsl --shutdown + restart, since .wslconfig is VM-boot-time only) and the instability stopped. Banked in reference_wsl_memory_config.md so a future session recognizes the symptom immediately instead of debugging code that isn't broken.

37. Three nulls in one night: Frac-connections closed, MQAR-refine architecturally capped, GDN-2/OSDN blocked on VRAM — strategic reshuffle to Sparse Delta Memory

2026-07-12

Frac-connections was the last of the three 350M gate items and the natural first data point of the session. The A/B treatment arm finished at 100M tok on the 5080: final val_ppl 88.54 vs control 86.69, a −1.85 ppl loss well past the ~1.5 ppl noise floor. Memory-Δ actually rose (+46.95), but overall model quality fell. Wiring was proven inert at flag=off (control matched NorMuon's control byte-for-byte on the same recipe), so this is a real null on the mechanism, not a plumbing bug. That closes negative — the third confirmed instance in this project of a residual-add-style trick failing to lift LM quality at 30M — and finishes the 350M gate at 2-of-3 (NorMuon ✓, Engram v2 ✓, Frac-connections ✗).

Test 2 verdict: MQAR-refine is architecturally capped, not budget-starved

The 500M post-pretrain refine pipeline had come back all-null in the prior session, and the leading hypothesis was budget — 50M refine tokens is ~1% of the 5B pretrain, at a modest auxiliary loss weight (0.5). Test 2 (mqar_refine_500m_strong, +200M tok at MQAR weight 3.0 — 4× the budget and 6× the weight) was launched to settle that hypothesis. It finished cleanly at 5300M tok in 7.05h.

Ran recall_eval on the final checkpoint vs the sparsity_refine_500m baseline the refine chain was seeded from. At N=4/8/16/30: accuracies 44/36/20/14% vs 45/36/20/14% (identical); mean rank 3560/3949/4738/6642 vs 3571/3958/4531/6699 (fluctuations within noise, no consistent direction). Under recall-dominant training at 4× the budget, recall did not move. The low-N recall null is architectural, not budget. MQAR-refine is a dead pipeline stage for this model class.

That verdict cuts across the whole enhancement queue. GDN-2 and OSDN attack the delta-rule write rule and are still worth screening — but the write rule alone can't move a state-capacity ceiling. The only backlog item that attacks capacity is Sparse Delta Memory (arXiv:2607.07386, Meta FAIR, Jul 8 2026): sparse addressing into a large explicit memory bank, scaling linear-RNN state orders of magnitude under an isoFLOP/iso-param constraint. Previously ranked as "secondary, gated on Test 2's outcome." Test 2's outcome pulled its gate. SDM promoted to top strategic priority.

GDN-2 and OSDN blocked on VRAM; strategic order shifts

The plan for the newly freed 5090 was to run the GDN-2 and OSDN treatments (control was already running on the 5080). Discovery: the sequential memory-loop autograd tape at d_mem=1024 × T=512 × 2 layers peaks around ~50 GB even at batch=4/grad_accum=8, spills to WDDM shared GPU memory on the 5090 (32 GB), and grinds. GDN-2 held ~32 GB VRAM + 5 GB shared for ~10 minutes without printing step 100. Killed and confirmed structural, not config — the sequential loop persists the M state at every timestep for BPTT, and at these dimensions there's no batch size small enough to make that comfortable. Fix path is a code change (gradient checkpointing on the per-timestep step, or committing the chunked WY kernel that was originally deferred until a screen wins). Both blocked until that lands.

Overnight chains: combined-bake validation on the 5090

Rather than leave the freed GPU idle overnight, launched a chain the queue actually needed: the first-ever combined NorMuon + Engram-v2 30M validation (screen_bake_normuon_engramv2_combined_30m). Both are confirmed wins, but they'd never been tested together — and the 350M restart bake will run them combined. The 5080 continues its seed0 control and chains a seed1 control after. Both chain scripts are file-based (pgrep-polled, no in-memory state, robust to WSL restart), sitting in temp/chain_5080_overnight.sh and temp/chain_5090_overnight.sh.

Also: an earlier heredoc-based watcher script (set up to auto-fire recall_eval when Test 2 hit its token target) had silently never launched its polling loop — the heredoc shell got stuck at setup and the whole watcher was a zombie. Test 2 finished 7h ago and the eval was only fired manually tonight when the leftover process was noticed. Chalked up as another vote for keeping chain scripts as standalone .sh files rather than nesting them inside heredocs of nested shells.

38. Same night: GDN-2/OSDN unblocked, LoRA SFT repaired, SDM scoped — and the combined-bake seed0 puts up the biggest memory-Δ this project has ever measured

2026-07-12

The three nulls from §37 left the 5090 idle after ~90 minutes of overnight chained work (combined-bake seed0 + seed1) and left the GDN-2/OSDN screens paused on a real code-level blocker (sequential-loop autograd tape). The rest of the session — while both bake runs cooked — was spent removing that blocker, repairing one more shelved item, and scoping the strategic reshuffle's top new priority.

Gradient checkpointing on the GDN-2/OSDN sequential memory loops

Root cause of the ~50 GB tape: SpikingHebbianBlock.forward_sequence's per-timestep loop for GDN-2 and OSDN persists roughly five tracked tensors of size (B, d_v, d_k) at every one of T=512 timesteps, over 2 layers. At d_mem=1024 that's ~5 × 512 × 2 × B × 1024² × 4 bytes ≈ 40 B GB, which is the observed number at batch=4. The chunked WY kernel was the eventual answer but explicitly deferred until a screen wins — and grad-ckpt is smaller in scope, lets us screen first, and is orthogonal to which write rule ends up chosen.

Refactored: extracted two module-level pure functions _gdn2_chunk_fwd and _osdn_chunk_fwd (per-chunk sequential loop, take state in and return state out); wrapped them in torch.utils.checkpoint(use_reentrant=False) per chunk of 32 timesteps when self.training, and called them directly in eval. Peak activation memory drops T/C = 16× for a ~2× backward compute hit — a cheap trade. Wrote temp/test_gdn2_osdn_gradckpt.py: (i) train-mode-with-ckpt is bit-exact to eval-mode-no-ckpt on forward output AND parameter-gradient signature (both cases: max|d|=0); (ii) flag-off regression (state_dict identical, forward bit-identical) still holds; (iii) CUDA VRAM check shows measurably lower peak. All 8 new checks pass, plus all 16 original oracle checks. Restored treatment configs to batch=32/grad_accum=1, matching the shared control — the earlier drop to batch=4 was purely to work around the tape blowup and is no longer needed.

LoRA SFT config fix

Separately, configs/sft_30m_lora.yaml had a dangling sft_init: screen_gated_delta_30m_mqar pointing at a run that was never actually made (the earlier launch attempt crashed at file-not-found). Repointed to screen_engram_v2_ab_control_30m — the closest existing 30M ckpt (seed-confirmed gated-delta baseline @ 100M tok, val_ppl 48.03) — and aligned the config's arch fields to that base (recurrent: false, fpt_K: 10, batch/accum matched down for VRAM). CPU smoke test: build model per SFT arch + load base state_dict → 0 missing / 0 unexpected keys. Ready to run.

5090 follow-on chain

Wrote temp/chain_5090_followon.sh, launched at 01:00. Watches for the primary combined-bake chain to finish (via pgrep -f on the config filenames plus the parent-chain script name — three orthogonal signals so a false-negative can't silently jump the queue), then runs, in order: GDN-2 treatment 30M → OSDN treatment 30M → LoRA SFT 30M. Each ~1.5–2h → ~5–6h of chained follow-on work. Bulletproof file-based watcher, no dependence on Claude polling — a lesson from the heredoc watcher that silently never launched its polling loop and cost 7 hours of stale data yesterday.

SDM design doc

Test 2's null established that the delta-rule state itself is the binding recall constraint at this scale. GDN-2 and OSDN attack the write rule; only Sparse Delta Memory attacks state capacity. Scoped it in docs/sdm_design.md: the paper (Cabannes et al., Meta FAIR, arXiv:2607.07386) replaces the dense (d_v × d_k) state with an N-slot bank, addressed via Product-Key Memory (outer-sum scores over √N × √N, top-K in O(√N)), with per-slot gated delta writes applied only to the top-W selected slots and reads from the top-R selected slots. Composition matrix: beta_floor, decay_gate, NorMuon, Engram v2 all compose orthogonally; GDN-2 and OSDN are mutually exclusive (all three are same-M write-rule variants). Sizing plan: 30M screen N=1024, W=R=4, ~6M added params; 500M restart N=16384, W=R=8, ~165M added memory-state params (35% of non-embedding budget, matching the paper's 8B ratio). 8-phase build plan with a fracconn-style dense-oracle reduction test before any GPU time. Risk register: dead-slot collapse (analog of router collapse in MoE), autocast top-k determinism, sparse-scatter gradient correctness, recurrent_depth interaction.

Combined-bake seed0: the biggest memory-Δ this project has ever produced

At ~step 3000/6100 of the first-ever NorMuon + Engram-v2 combined 30M run: val_ppl 93.54 (still descending), memory-Δ +168.93 — well above the +94.44 the beta-fix produced at the same recipe scale, and unlike anything we've seen at 30M before. If it holds through end of run AND survives seed1 (chained), that's the strongest evidence yet that the two confirmed wins compose without interfering — which was the entire premise for baking them together into the next 500M. Interpretation caveat: memory-Δ is a diagnostic, not a fitness signal; the val_ppl trajectory is what matters. Morning check will settle it.

39. The combined bake wins decisively at 30M (both seeds) and promotes to 90M; GDN-2's outlier memory-Δ traced to per-channel gating, not a bug

2026-07-12 (later same session)

The 30M NorMuon + Engram-v2 combined-bake chain finished both seeds cleanly overnight. Final numbers: seed0 val_ppl 79.06 vs the plain gated-delta control's 81.87 (−2.8 ppl); seed1 val_ppl 68.22 vs the control's 80.03 (−11.8 ppl). Both seeds beat control, and by a wide enough margin on seed1 that this clears the bar the project's seed-noise pattern usually catches (MoE, Engram v1, and uniform recurrent-depth all had single-seed "wins" that reversed on reseed — this one holds in the same direction on both). Verdict: NorMuon and Engram v2 compose without interfering — the entire premise the 500M restart plan depends on. Promoted straight to a 90M validation rather than treating the 30M pair as sufficient on its own, per the project's standing 30M→90M→500M gate.

90M combined-bake launched

Built configs/bake_normuon_engramv2_combined_90m.yaml as a direct promotion of the 30M recipe: n_neurons 1024→2048, n_layers 2→4 (matching the existing 90M MoR configs' scale-up convention), d_mem unchanged at 1024, batch 32×1 → 16×2 (same effective batch, smaller per-step footprint for the 5080's 16GB), tokens 100M→400M. CPU-verified the config builds cleanly (80.27M params) before spending GPU time. Chained seed0→seed1 via temp/chain_5080_90m_bake.sh on the 5080, launched and confirmed stepping (10.5k+ tok/s, comfortable VRAM headroom at 8.4/16GB).

GDN-2's anomalous +313.65 memory-Δ: investigated, not a bug

The GDN-2 30M treatment's mid-run eval showed memory-Δ +313.65 — roughly 3× anything else measured this session, val_ppl already 80.50 at 66% through the run (already better than the plain-delta control's final 81.87). Two checks before trusting it: (1) confirmed the code's ablate_memory guard sits above the GDN-2 branch in forward_sequence, so the ablation path correctly skips the whole GDN-2 memory pathway — not a code-level miss. (2) Pulled NVIDIA's own reference implementation (github.com/NVlabs/GatedDeltaNet-2, lit_gpt/gdn2.py) to compare initialization: their erase/write gate projections use no bias with Xavier-uniform gain=2−2.5 (small, tight-spread init) and their decay uses per-head log-rate sampled uniform in [1,16] plus inverse-softplus dt_bias — both meaningfully different from our defaults (plain nn.Linear with bias, gdn2_a zero-init, W_fdecay.bias=−3 giving slow ~0.952 per-step decay). But the plain gated-delta control's own seed1 had already put up memory-Δ +211.25 on the exact same slow-decay-biased init — so GDN-2's +313.65 reads as roughly 1.5× the control's own high-end seed variance, consistent with per-channel gates concentrating more signal into the memory pathway than a scalar gate, not a broken ablation. Decision: let the run continue as-is. The NVIDIA-vs-ours init gap is banked as a real follow-up (an init-matching A/B, only if GDN-2 clears its own gate first) rather than treated as a blocker.

LoRA SFT: config verified fixed, stale error log is a red herring

A status check mid-session found logs_sft_30m_lora.txt still showing the original FileNotFoundError: no base ckpt found for sft_init=screen_gated_delta_30m_mqar — alarming at first glance since that error was supposedly fixed hours earlier. Traced it: the file is a stale leftover from the very first (pre-fix) launch attempt: the 5090 follow-on chain hadn't reached the LoRA-SFT step yet (GDN-2 still running ahead of it in the queue), so nothing had overwritten the old log. Re-checked configs/sft_30m_lora.yaml directly — the fix (sft_init: screen_engram_v2_ab_control_30m, arch fields aligned) is intact on disk. No action needed; noted as a reminder that a stale log file is a distinct failure mode from a live one, and worth an explicit timestamp/process-liveness check before treating old log content as current.

41. F1 confirmed at 30M (−2.7 ppl vs combined-bake control); SDM phase 3 wiring + grad-ckpt fix; F2 null; 90M combined-bake seed1 confirms composability

2026-07-13

The overnight AFK produced four decisions and three code changes:

F1 (muon_fix_gate_matrices) wins decisively at 30M. Final val_ppl 76.33 vs the combined-bake control's 79.06 (−2.7 ppl), well past the ~1.5 ppl noise floor. The new pre-floor β / α-clamp telemetry landed the day before immediately paid off: F1's β read at_floor=0-51% across the run (blk0 climbed late, blk1 stayed at 0), and α read lo_frac=33-36% for blk0 / 0-4% for blk1 — the gates were actively modulating instead of stuck at floor. Compare to the 90M combined-bake seed1 finishing the same night at val_ppl 46.98 with β at_floor=100% COLLAPSED and α lo_frac=100% FROZEN_LO on every one of its 4 blocks. F1 is BOTH the mechanistic fix AND a quality win. 500M restart MUST include muon_fix_gate_matrices: true, no longer a hypothesis.
F2 (muon_route_embeddings_by_type) is a null. Final val_ppl 79.58 vs control 79.06 (+0.5, well within noise). Critically, F2 alone did NOT unlock gate modulation — β read at_floor=100% COLLAPSED and α lo_frac=100% FROZEN_LO throughout, identical to the untreated combined bake. F1 is doing the real work; F2 is cosmetic (route the sparse-gradient bucket Embedding correctly by type). Include F2 for cleanliness at 500M but expect no quality signal from it. F1+F2 combined bake is currently running on the 5080 (~66% through as of handoff); tracking F1's trajectory as expected.

SDM phase 3 wiring landed same day, ahead of the deferral originally planned. Re-examined the earlier "wait until seed1 subprocess launches" deferral: model.py edits only affect FUTURE Python subprocesses, and the 5080's 90M seed1 wasn't due to launch for ~3.7h — ample buffer to land the change + verify. sdm_enabled wired into SpikingHebbianBlock + SpikingHebbianLM, plumbing in build_model + recall_eval.build. Simplification vs the original design-doc plan: sdm_N == d_mem enforced (raises otherwise), so the state stays square (N, N) and the existing state allocator works unchanged — matches the 30M screen's already-scoped sizing exactly; the 500M's non-square sizing (N=16384, d_mem=2048) needs a separate future refactor. Verified 21/21 in temp/test_sdm_wiring.py. 30M A/B config landed at 46.43M params vs control's 44.07M (+2.36M, less than the original ~3M/layer estimate because β/α gates are reused not duplicated).

Then SDM seed0 OOM'd at step 1. First 5090 launch of screen_sdm_ab_treatment_30m.yaml crashed with CUDA OOM at M = a * M in sdm.py:178, 128 MB alloc requested but 45.63 GB already allocated by PyTorch. Same pathology GDN-2/OSDN hit before their grad-checkpointing fix: the per-timestep loop persists a (B, N, d) = (32, 1024, 1024) = 128 MB M tensor in the autograd tape at every one of T=512 timesteps — ~65 GB total tape. Applied the exact same fix pattern used on GDN-2/OSDN: extracted _sdm_chunk_fwd as a module-level pure function, wrapped in checkpoint(use_reentrant=False) per chunk of 32. Peak memory drops ~T/32=16× for a ~2× backward compute hit. Bit-exact vs eager path (train-mode-with-ckpt output == eval-mode-no-ckpt output, verified in temp/test_sdm.py, 27/27 checks including the new grad-ckpt equivalence). Standing rule: any new sequential per-t loop over a large (B, dim, dim) state MUST grad-checkpoint from day 1. SDM seed1 relaunched cleanly, currently running on the 5090.

Also this session: 90M combined-bake seed1 finished decisively (val_ppl 46.98, memory-Δ +122.76) — the seed0/seed1 pair (48.14 and 46.98) confirms NorMuon+Engram-v2 composability at 90M scale, the last gate before the 500M restart plan. GDN-2 30M seed1 confirm ran into a CUDA-unknown-error mid-run on the 5080 (~1400 steps in, thermal/driver hiccup); relaunched then killed to move to the 5090 (16GB spill was costing throughput). It'll fire on the 5090 after SDM seed1 via a new chain_5090_followon4.sh. LoRA SFT 30M completed cleanly (final loss 4.34, 20 min). And a fourth compositionality bake (F1 + F2 stacked) is running on the 5080 to test whether the two fixes interact.

40. Code review lands 11 findings; SDM phase 2 built & CPU-verified; GDN-2 wins the 30M gate; β-collapse-under-floor caught live in the 90M bake

2026-07-12 (mid-day, AFK-window session)

A deep architecture/roadmap review pass (see docs/to-do.html for the ranked finding list) produced 11 findings across three severity bands. Four applied this session as pure-additive changes safe to run alongside the mid-flight 5080 and 5090 chains. Two mattered enough to change the story going forward — and one of them turned out to be the reason a live 90M eval line looked odd.

F3 landed — pre-floor β telemetry, restoring the collapse-pressure signal that beta_floor: 0.1 silently disabled. The standing "watch dead_frac climbing during a run" rule was mathematically incapable of firing under the recipe default: β = floor + (1−floor)·σ(W_β), so β ≥ 0.1 by construction and dead_frac (frac below 0.01) is 0 always. Journey §36's "revived by refine (dead=0% on all blocks)" claim was circular. Added at_floor_frac: invert the floor to recover σ = (β−floor)/(1−floor), count σ < 0.01 — the true collapse indicator. Same 0.95 rule now applies to it. Also added _alpha_stats for the analog silent-failure of the decay-gate clamp: sigmoid(W_α)·clamp(0.5, 0.9999) freezes at either boundary with zero gradient there; hi_frac at 0.9999 = never-forget (unbounded memory accumulation), lo_frac at 0.5 = fast-forget (delta can't build long-range bindings).

F3 caught a live case immediately, in the 90M combined bake seed0. By step 6000-ish, β on all 4 blocks read μ=0.10 (=== floor) with the legacy dead_frac at 0% — the μ=floor exact-match tells you the pre-floor sigmoid saturated across the board. The run is still doing well (memory-Δ climbing +38 → +48.6, val_ppl 95 → 56 at 52% budget), but this means the delta-rule β modulation is functionally constant. The "combined bake win" being validated is really NorMuon-optimized constant-β delta + Engram v2, not β-modulated delta + Engram v2 as the composition claim assumed. This is worth banking as an interpretation caveat on the 90M verdict when it lands.

Root-cause candidate: F1 (Muon on 1-row gate matrices). Newton-Schulz on a (1, N) matrix reduces to direction-normalization — the update has constant magnitude regardless of gradient magnitude, so a 1-row sigmoid-fed gate like W_beta or W_alpha gets pushed at a fixed step per iteration and easily lands in a saturated regime where it stays. The fix_gate_matrices flag in src/muon.py exists to route these to AdamW instead, but its default is False for resume-compatibility with pre-2026-07-06 checkpoints, and no config sets it. Every current run — the 90M bake included — is training W_beta/W_alpha through this pathology. The beta_floor recipe fix treats the symptom; F1 addresses the underlying cause. The 500M restart MUST set muon_fix_gate_matrices: true or the restart bakes in a known-dead β on the thesis-critical delta rule.

Other applied findings. F10: patched scripts/recall_eval.py's build() to forward the missing beta, eta, ponder_*, recurrent_deep_supervision, recurrent_pass_weights kwargs — same silent-wrong-arch class already bitten twice in the project's history (engram_factual_eval.py, and recall_eval itself in the Test 2 diagnosis). Also verified build against the live 500M and 90M combined-bake configs. F4 + F6: SDM design-doc amendments. The 500M's use_fla_kernel: true setting means delta_fla_gated splits d_mem=2048 into 8×256² diagonal blocks (fla's head-dim cap of 256) — effective per-layer memory state is ~524K params, ~8× less than the notional dense 4.19M. Test 2's "architectural recall ceiling" verdict is *even more* of a state-capacity constraint than an ambient reading suggested; strengthens the SDM case rather than weakens it. Composition matrix corrected: GDN-2/OSDN are per-slot write-rule variants of SDM's per-slot update (both port unchanged), NOT mutually exclusive as originally framed — if either wins seed1, the right move is a phase-6 sdm_write_rule: gated_delta | gdn2 | osdn arm inside SDM rather than a competitive screen.

SDM phase 2 built and CPU-verified in the same session — the top strategic priority item (Sparse Delta Memory, arXiv:2607.07386, promoted after Test 2's architectural-ceiling verdict) went from paper-mapped-scope to a working module. src/sdm.py: PKMIndex (outer-sum scoring, per-sub top-M candidate + global top-K, O(K·√N)) and SparseSDMMemory (owns M_0 zero-init + separate write/read PKM heads; reuses the calling block's β/α/v so beta_floor telemetry keeps working unchanged; per-slot selection-frequency counter for dead-slot detection). Fracconn-style CPU battery in temp/test_sdm.py: 22/22 checks pass — PKM outer-sum invariant, PKM top-K vs dense oracle at K∈{1,4,8}, SDM zero-init exact-zero (safe residual drop-in), β=0 closed-form deterministic reads, α=0 fast-forget correctness, grad flow through M_0 and both PKM heads, determinism, autocast bf16 finiteness, slot-stats populated.

The design doc's "dense_all_slots reduces exactly to gated delta rule" phrasing turned out to be aspirational and false: SDM's per-slot (N, d) memory is a structurally different object from delta's (d_v, d_k) shared-outer-product state at every parameter setting. The identity checks landed instead prove correctness without a reduce-to-baseline oracle, in the fracconn spirit. Design doc updated to reflect this.

SDM phase 3 (model.py wiring) deliberately deferred to the next session. The 5080 90M bake seed1 will fire on a fresh Python subprocess after seed0 completes and will re-import model.py. Even flag-off-safe SDM plumbing introduces new code paths that risk seed0/seed1 A/B fairness contamination — and that A/B is the primary gate for the 500M restart decision. Same discipline the GDN-2/OSDN patches followed. Once the seed1 subprocess is running, its model.py is cached and phase-3 edits are safe for that run.

Meanwhile on GPUs. GDN-2 30M treatment finished with a decisive win: val_ppl 76.98 vs control 81.87 (−4.9 ppl, well past the ~1.5 ppl noise floor), memory-Δ +304.5 stable, both β blocks healthy (μ=0.53/0.59 — not floor-saturated, unlike the 90M bake). Clears both bar conditions from docs/gdn2_osdn_design.md; queue seed1 confirm before touching OSDN/LoRA outcomes. OSDN 30M started immediately after and is at step 400/6103 (~4h remaining). The 5090 chain is running down its queue as designed.

Findings queued for next session (not applied this session): F1 (Muon fix_gate_matrices for the 500M restart), F2 (Engram-v2 bucket table lands in Muon instead of AdamW — needs 30M A/B), F5 (define 500M restart trigger to include SDM 30M verdict, not just 90M bake confirm), F7 (chunked-kernel port for GDN-2/OSDN — only if screens win seed1), F8 (confidence-gated inference replay), F9 (dtype match on GDN-2/OSDN chunk fns if they promote to 90M).

Major version: 1.24 (Code-review pass landed 11 findings, 4 applied this session as safe additive changes: F3 pre-floor β telemetry restores the collapse-pressure signal beta_floor had silently disabled and immediately caught a live case — the 90M bake's β is functionally constant at floor on all 4 blocks, plausibly caused by F1 (Muon on 1-row gate matrices degenerates to constant-magnitude updates); F11 α clamp-boundary telemetry; F10 recall_eval.build() missing kwargs; F4+F6 SDM design-doc amendments (500M's fla splits d_mem into diagonal blocks => ~8× less effective state than dense; GDN-2/OSDN reframed as per-slot write-rule variants of SDM, not mutually exclusive). SDM phase 2 built & CPU-verified 22/22: src/sdm.py with PKMIndex and SparseSDMMemory. Phase 3 wiring deferred behind 5080 seed1 for A/B fairness.
