42. GDN-2 decisively beats SDM; a Batch-A recipe gap found and closed; GDN-2/OSDN chunk-size perf fix; MoR's 200M test sequenced before the 500M restart

2026-07-15

GDN-2 vs SDM: decided. The full-recipe composition test (NorMuon+Engram-v2+F1+F2+GDN-2+curriculum) finished at 72.26 val_ppl — the best composition result yet, beating GDN-2-alone's 76.98. SDM's equivalent composition test came in at 79.66, worse than the no-memory-rule combined bake's 76.88. SDM+F1 standalone also trailed badly (memory-Δ +15–33 vs GDN-2's +233–280) even after F1 unstuck its β-collapse (β went from 100%-COLLAPSED to μ=0.30, but the memory signal never followed). GDN-2 is the memory-rule for the 500M restart. OSDN stays deprioritized separately (seed0 −1.31ppl vs control, under the noise floor, and the slowest recipe in the family at ~20h/100M-tok).

Audit found a real gap before committing to the restart: Batch A was never re-tested with this week's recipe. RMSNorm + z-loss + deeper-MTP + embed_scale (with the paired small-init fix, see §Batch A chapter) was the single biggest 30M win in the project's history — −32.6% val_ppl — and was baked into the old demo_phase1b_500m_v2_fast checkpoint. But it was never carried into any of the NorMuon/Engram-v2/F1/F2/GDN-2/curriculum screens that produced this week's recipe. Built screen_bake_full_gdn2_batchA_30m.yaml and screen_bake_500m_derisk_gdn2_batchA_90m.yaml (full recipe + Batch A, 30M and 90M) and launched both in parallel rather than gating one behind the other — Batch A's win was already large and re-validated twice, so the parallel bet was judged worth the wall-clock savings before an 11+ day restart.

Same audit pass found both 90M de-risk configs (screen_bake_500m_derisk_gdn2_90m/_sdm_90m) were missing curriculum_enabled — built before curriculum's seed1 confirmed (two-seed win, 45.15/46.72 vs control 48.03). Fixed both; the just-finished 72.26 combined-bake result also predates the curriculum fix, so the 90M Batch-A de-risk run is the first to carry the fully complete recipe.

GDN-2 is launch-bound, not compute-bound — found via a live util check. The 90M Batch-A de-risk sat at 50% GPU utilization despite 28.4/32GB VRAM used at batch=32. Root cause: _GDN2_OSDN_CHUNK (the grad-checkpoint chunk size for GDN-2/OSDN's sequential memory loop) is hardcoded at 32 in src/model.py, meaning the sequential Python-loop/kernel-launch overhead dominates regardless of batch size. Bumped to 64, halving the launch count — bit-exact (checkpointing only changes the backward recompute schedule, not the forward math), but roughly doubles VRAM for the tracked tensor (~4GB→~8GB at batch=32). The live 90M run was killed to pick up the change and needs batch_size dropped to ~16–24 before relaunch to avoid OOM.

GDN-2's cost was weighed explicitly against dropping it. GDN-2 runs ~2× slower than a no-memory-rule recipe (~1100 vs ~2500 tok/s at 90M) — the 500M restart goes from ~11 days to ~22+ days. Considered switching to SDM for a smaller quality lift at higher speed, but the data doesn't support it: SDM isn't a smaller version of GDN-2's win, it's a net negative relative to no memory rule at all. Glenn's call: eat the 2× slowdown for GDN-2's quality win rather than downgrade.

MoR gets a real test, sequenced before the restart, not folded in blind. The project's prior MoR work (see Engram/adaptive-compute rebuild and its follow-ups) found the paper's own literature places MoR's underperform-vanilla crossover at ~135M params — every 30M/90M screen this project ran was structurally too small to trust either direction. Rather than skip MoR or gamble it into the 500M untested, built screen_mor_200m_2b.yaml (~244M params, 2B tokens, current recipe minus Batch A/GDN-2-vs-SDM decision baked in) to cross that threshold with a real token budget, sequenced before the 500M restart (~3–5 days) rather than in parallel — a clean answer before an 11+ day commitment beats a mid-flight kill/restart decision. Not yet launched; needs Batch A folded in if that composition confirms, same as the 500M restart config itself (screen_500m_restart_gdn2.yaml, built, also not yet updated with Batch A).

Also this session: a full reorganization of 217 log files and 171 configs into logs/ + logs/archive/ (185 historical) + configs/archive/ (134 historical), protecting the active/running set and forward-planned-but-unrun drafts (which turned out to include the exact de-risk/combined-bake configs this chapter needed). Separately, a 45GB cleanup of models/brains/ — per-eval diagnostic .brain snapshots (distinct from the real .pt checkpoints, which were untouched) for 123 completed/superseded screens.

Major version: 1.26 (GDN-2 DECIDED as the 500M memory-rule, beating SDM decisively in both standalone and full-recipe composition (72.26 vs 79.66). Batch A recipe gap found and closed — the project's biggest 30M win was never re-tested with this week's recipe; now screening at 30M+90M in parallel. _GDN2_OSDN_CHUNK perf fix (32→64) halves GDN-2's launch-bound sequential-loop overhead, bit-exact. MoR's 200M/2B-token test sequenced before the 500M restart per Glenn's explicit call. 500M restart config built, not yet launched. logs/configs reorganized (185+134 archived); 45GB models/brains/ snapshot cleanup.)

43. F7 done, exceeded scope: GDN-2's Triton kernel investigated and rejected, exact dense chunked-WY kernel built instead — 500M restart named 500M_v3

2026-07-15 (evening)

fla's Triton GDN-2 kernel (F7) was the obvious speed fix — and it's the wrong one. fla.ops.gdn2.chunk_gdn2 hard-caps key head-dim at 256, forcing our dense d_mem×d_mem state into block-diagonal multi-head splitting. Measured directly (not inferred): MQAR recall accuracy at N=16 goes 91% (dense, H=1) → 70% (H=4, the kernel's forced minimum at 30M) → 11% (H=8) → 8% (H=16) — a cliff after H=4, not a graceful decline. Val_ppl and the ablation-Δ proxy stayed fine or improved through the whole collapse; only the real MQAR eval caught it. A same-weights loop-vs-kernel isolation test confirmed this is architectural fragmentation, not a fixable kernel-precision bug (bf16/fp16/fp32 kernel inputs gave nearly identical results). The 500M config's d_mem=2048 forces H=8 — using the fla kernel as-is would have shipped a fast, great-perplexity, essentially non-recalling 500M model.

Built the exact alternative instead: a dense chunked-WY GEMM reformulation (gdn2_chunked_dense in src/delta_chunked.py), matching the same factorization family fla's own kernels use internally but with GLA-style two-level anchored gating so every exponential stays ≤1 — no head splitting, no SRAM cap. Three real bugs surfaced only through actual training runs, not synthetic parity (all now covered by a hardened test suite): autocast silently downcasting the internal fp32 GEMMs to bf16 (NaN by step 100, fixed with a hard autocast-disable inside the function); a midpoint-shift exponential factorization overflowing/zeroing on trained fast-decay channels (NaN by step 300, rewritten as the two-level anchored form); and unit_lower_inv's Neumann-doubling inverse blowing up numerically on trained MQAR-correlated keys (swapped to torch.linalg.solve_triangular).

30M validation confirmed a clean win. Same recipe as the dense-loop champion (val_ppl 76.98, MQAR N=16 91%/rank3): the new kernel reached val_ppl 77.03, memory-Δ +299.70 vs +304.54, MQAR recall 89%/rank2 at N=16 (98/98/89/38/19% across N=4–64, matching or beating the champion at every N) — full recall preserved, at ~23× the champion's training speed (23.2k vs ~1k tok/s). Wired behind a gdn2_chunked config flag, default true; the original loop stays as fallback. The 500M restart is named 500M_v3 and its config must set gdn2_chunked: true — full dense state preserved, GDN-2's ~2× pretraining tax eliminated, no recall regression.

Major version: 1.27 (F7 done — fla's Triton GDN-2 kernel investigated and rejected after its 256-headdim cap was found to collapse MQAR recall at H≥8 (91%→70%→11%→8% at N=16, confirmed architectural not precision-fixable). Built an exact dense chunked-WY GEMM kernel instead, fixing 3 real bugs found via live training (autocast downcast, exponential overflow, unstable triangular inverse). 30M-validated: same ppl/recall as the dense-loop champion at ~23x training speed. 500M restart named 500M_v3, must set gdn2_chunked: true.)

44. Batch A confirmed (2-seed + recall), MoR killed (Stage 2 never got its speedup), Engram heads inconclusive — 500M v3 cleared to launch

2026-07-16

Batch A is now a fully confirmed win, not just a promising 30M number. The composition gap found in the previous chapter resolved cleanly. 30M seed0: val_ppl 58.09 vs the no-Batch-A control's 76.98 (−24.5%). 30M seed1: 57.36 — held close to seed0, unlike every prior single-seed "win" this project has chased (MoE, Engram v1, uniform recurrent-depth all reversed on reseed; this one didn't. recall_eval.py (MQAR) confirms the win is a real memory improvement, not just a perplexity artifact: Batch A beats the control at every N, most dramatically at N=32 (64% vs 47% accuracy, mean rank 10 vs 103). An initial worry — memory-Δ ratio (ablated/full ppl) compressed from ~5x to ~3.7x under Batch A — turned out to be a false alarm once recall was actually measured: the non-memory pathway improved even more than the memory pathway, not at its expense. The 90M de-risk (screen_bake_500m_derisk_gdn2_batchA_90m.yaml) confirmed composition holds at 4-layer scale, and was moved from the 5080 to the freed-up 5090 mid-run via a checkpoint-resume chain script (kill after a checkpoint save, relaunch the identical config on the other GPU — train_gpu.py's resume is keyed by run_name only, not GPU, so this just works). Batch A's fields (use_rmsnorm, embed_scale, z_loss_weight, mtp_*) are now folded into the actual 500M config.
MoR's 200M/2B test (queued in the previous chapter) got killed after step 200 — and the root cause reframes the whole recurrent-depth arc's ending. Throughput was ~2295 tok/s, projecting 7.7 days for a 2B-token budget; Glenn killed it as impractical rather than tie up the 5090 for a week on a maybe-marginal result. Digging into why it was that slow found something worth recording: Stage 2's RecurrentDepthRouter (built back in the adaptive-compute rebuild) was only ever implemented as the dense/masked variant — every token gets the FULL extra recurrent pass computed regardless of what the router decides, with the routing decision only applied as a post-hoc mask/merge. The sparse-gather optimization that would give MoR its actual FLOP saving (only compute the extra pass for tokens the router keeps) was scoped from day one but never built. So MoR as implemented has never had any speed advantage over uniform depth-2 looping — it's pure 2× pass cost plus router overhead. Combined with uniform/fixed-depth recursion already being closed negative, 2-seed-confirmed, all recursion work is now deferred to a documented future Phase 3 (post-500M-v3): build the sparse-gather Phase 2 first, then re-screen MoR at a token budget the paper's own data says is meaningful (≥135M params, ~0.9–2.3B tokens), rather than repeat an under-scaled or artificially-slow test again.

Engram-v2's head-count wasn't as settled as the recipe assumed. The seed-confirmed win was measured at engram_v2_heads=2; the recipe had silently drifted to 4 with no documented ablation showing 4 beats 2. A 4-vs-8 A/B at 30M (after extending src/engram_v2.py's _PRIMES tuple from 20 to 32 entries — heads=8 needs 3×8=24 hash primes, more than existed) came back essentially tied on training-log metrics (val_ppl 57.95 vs 57.57, spike 18.5% vs 18.1%, memory-Δ +147 vs +150). engram_factual_eval.py reported both as "Engram harmful" — Engram ON scoring worse than OFF, contradicting the original seed-confirmed result outright. Traced to a real bug, not a real regression: the eval script's model-builder predates Batch A and doesn't pass through mtp_enabled/MTP kwargs, so it silently drops mtp_norm/mtp_proj weights via strict=False on load and scores a different (no-MTP) architecture than the one that was actually trained. Both arms hit the bug identically, so the relative 4-vs-8 comparison should still be sound even though the absolute "harmful" verdict isn't trusted. Kept heads=4 in the 500M config — heads=8's edge was too small, on too broken a metric, to justify the dependency for an 11-day run. The eval script itself needs a fix before its results can be trusted on any Batch-A-era checkpoint; not done yet.

500M v3 cleared its pre-launch smoke test clean. Full architecture (479.35M params, all of Batch A + Engram-v2 + GDN-2 chunked + curriculum + MTP composed together for the first time at true scale) built, trained, evaluated, and checkpointed without error. The one result worth flagging: β stayed healthy across all 5 blocks (μ≈0.55, 0% dead, 0% at_floor everywhere) — the Muon gate-matrix fix (F1) that mattered so much at 90M holds at full depth too. Two loose ends before the real launch: a sparsity_lambda A/B (1.5 vs 3.0, vs the recipe's default 0.5) is running to see whether a tighter sparsity pull can bring the ~15–20% observed spike rate closer to the biological/story-friendly range without giving back quality or recall — motivated by Glenn's plan to start posting about the project publicly once 500M lands, where a lower, more defensible spike rate strengthens the pitch. And the smoke test itself left a real checkpoint (models/checkpoints/500m_v3/step_0000313.pt) that train_gpu.py would silently resume from — it reads whatever the latest checkpoint in the run's directory is regardless of how it got there — corrupting the real run's step count and LR schedule if not deleted first. Also renamed screen_500m_v3.yaml → 500m_v3.yaml (and its run_name) under a new convention: screen_ is now reserved for configs that test/screen a feature, not for the actual run being committed to.

Major version: 1.28 (Batch A CONFIRMED — 2-seed 30M win (58.09/57.36 vs 76.98 control) plus a real recall_eval win at every N, 90M de-risk holds, folded into the 500M config. MoR's 200M test killed for impracticality (~2295 tok/s); root cause found and recorded: Stage 2 never got the sparse-gather optimization that would have made it fast, so it never had any FLOP advantage over uniform looping — all recursion work deferred to a documented future Phase 3. Engram-v2 heads=4-vs-8 A/B inconclusive; found and diagnosed (not yet fixed) a bug in engram_factual_eval.py that silently drops MTP weights on Batch-A-era checkpoints. 500M v3 smoke-tested clean at true 479M-param scale, β healthy across all 5 blocks. New naming convention: screen_ prefix reserved for test configs only.)

45. A real torch.compile bug found and fixed (3×), a promising MQAR trick rejected on recall, block_size raised via a VRAM probe — 500M v3 LAUNCHES

2026-07-17

Pre-launch audit found five real issues before a single token trained. A dedicated review pass (files, config, and the launch script) turned up: the leftover smoke-test checkpoint 500m_v3/step_0000313.pt that train_gpu.py would have silently resumed from (flagged in the previous chapter, now actually deleted); engram_factual_eval.py's build() drift, which turned out to be worse than suspected — it also omitted gdn2_enabled, so a Batch-A/GDN-2 checkpoint was being scored with ~45×-attenuated embeddings and the wrong memory kernel entirely, not just missing MTP weights; an off-by-len(orders) undercounting bug in EngramV2's prime-count assert that would have silently let heads≥6 wrap and reuse hash multipliers; unbounded models/brains/500m_v3/ snapshot growth (~13GB over the full run, now snapshot_brain: false); and crash-resume not advancing the data RNG or CurriculumStream's counter, meaning a mid-run crash would have replayed the same random windows from step 0. All five fixed and verified — the Engram eval fix was re-run on the actual seed0 Batch-A checkpoint and now shows Engram ON beating OFF cleanly (log-prob +0.008, rank −65.6, top-10 +2.5pp), confirming the earlier "harmful" verdict was purely the broken eval, not a real regression.

torch.compile finally works — a genuine 3× speedup, and it took finding a real miscompile bug to get there. Live throughput was ~3,900 tok/s, projecting 14 days for the 5B-token run; Glenn asked for a serious throughput push rather than accepting it. Chunk-size tuning in gdn2_chunked_dense turned out to be a dead end (32–512 all measured within noise except 32 itself, which is a real cliff — confirming the earlier 32→64 fix from two chapters back), and MQAR-augmented training was found to be the dominant hidden cost: a full second forward pass every step, ~37–59% of total step time on its own. Revisiting compile=true (previously abandoned, see the Tier-1 speedups chapter) with fresh research found the actual root cause of every prior compile attempt's mysterious slowness or quality loss: lif_parallel()'s FPT fixed-point convergence loop uses data-dependent control flow (torch.equal(...) + early break) and a straight-through surrogate gradient, both of which get silently miscompiled — the forward loss looks completely normal, but spike rate quietly collapses (0.185→0.11) and val_ppl comes out ~2× worse. A single @torch.compiler.disable on that one function, leaving the much larger GDN-2 kernel/projections/head compiled, fixed it completely: 30M val_ppl 58.18 vs eager 58.09, memory-Δ and spike rate both matched. Combined with emulate_precision_casts (preserving the bf16↔fp32 casts at the GDN-2 kernel's autocast(False) boundary, which inductor otherwise elides across) and moving MTP logits from a side-effect attribute to a return value (attribute writes don't reliably propagate out of a compiled forward), whole-model compile went from silently broken to numerically exact.
The fast compile mode crashed the live run — traced to an open upstream PyTorch bug, not our code. reduce-overhead (CUDA Graph capture) measured 3.63× vs eager at real 500M shape, faster than default's 3.14×. It crashed on the first loss.backward() of the actual launch: RuntimeError: ... accessing tensor output of CUDAGraphs that has been overwritten by a subsequent run. Research found this is a confirmed, currently-unfixed bug in torch 2.6.0–2.9.1 (pytorch#169545, pytorch#148829) — CUDA Graph Trees' static output buffers don't compose safely with grad_accum microsteps plus torch.utils.checkpoint recompute during backward (both present in the GDN-2 kernel), and the error message's own suggested fixes are proven insufficient in the upstream repro. Shipped compile_mode: default instead — costs ~13% of peak speed for eliminating crash risk on an unattended multi-day run, the right trade.

MQAR itself got a second, separate compiled wrapper (mode="default", dynamic=True, deliberately never CUDA Graphs given the crash above) rather than running through the main compiled model — its sequence length varies every call. Validated at 30M: val_ppl 58.05 vs 58.09 eager, memory-Δ 163 vs 156, recall_eval within B=64 sampling noise of the eager-MQAR baseline. The gain was smaller than hoped, ~10–15% over eager MQAR rather than the main path's 3×, because MQAR's short synthetic sequence is itself launch-bound and doesn't compile away as cleanly as a full T=512+ forward.

A tempting MQAR-cadence trick was tried and rejected — general perplexity improving while recall got worse. Firing MQAR every 2nd step at 2× weight (same expected gradient, cheaper) looked like a huge win on val_ppl alone: 37.51 at 30M, confirmed across both seeds, vs the eager-every-step baseline's 58.09. But recall_eval told a different story — a real, consistent recall regression at high N (N=32 accuracy 56%→36%, N=64 34%→17%, both seeds). This is exactly the failure mode the project's memory-first design exists to catch: general capability improving at the direct expense of the in-context recall pathway. Left at the default mqar_cadence: 1 (every step); not revisited without first understanding why halving frequency specifically hurts recall rather than capability broadly.

block_size raised 512→640 as a genuine quality lever, not just a speed one — longer context gives the Hebbian memory more room to bind against. A VRAM probe at true 500M shape with real optimizers and both compiled wrappers (temp/bench_blocksize.py) swept 512→1024 in 64-token steps targeting ≤90% of the 5090's 32GB: 640 was the ceiling (28.58GB; 704 pushed to 30.51GB, rejected). Confirmed via a 30M screen at the new block_size before trusting 5 days of GPU time to it: no crash, spike rate matched the T=512 baseline (0.18 vs 0.185), memory-Δ was if anything stronger (184–238 vs 156) with more context available. The higher raw val_ppl at T=640 for the same 100M-token budget (71.48 vs 58.09) is an artifact of fewer optimizer steps per token at longer T, not a quality regression — the LR schedule is computed as a fraction of total_steps, so it reshapes correctly for the real 5B-token run regardless of block_size.

500M v3 is LIVE. Launched with the full stack: compile (default mode) + the LIF fix + the separate MQAR-compile wrapper + block_size: 640 + batch_size: 15. Two live-launch scares, both resolved without losing progress: a Windows auto-update reboot killed the process outright (no checkpoint lost, nothing had saved yet — just relaunched), and dedicated VRAM crept to 31.4GB with 1.6GB spilling into Windows/WDDM shared memory, silently dropping GPU util 97%→70% with no error — fixed by dropping batch_size 16→15 (the live run needs more headroom than the synthetic probe's 3-microstep sample suggested). A third scare — ~13 minutes of 0% GPU util and climbing VRAM right after each launch — turned out to be normal: whole-model + separate-MQAR-wrapper compile at 500M/5-layer scale takes far longer to trace than the 30M screens' ~1–2 minutes, and CPU usage stayed high throughout (tracing, not hung) until util jumped to 97%+ and real steps started. Live measured throughput settled at ~11,343 tok/s — in line with the compile validation's predictions, and roughly 2.9× the original 3,900 tok/s, cutting the 5B-token run from ~14 days to roughly 5.

Major version: 1.29 (Found and fixed the real reason every prior torch.compile attempt on this codebase looked broken or unhelpful: lif_parallel's data-dependent FPT loop + straight-through surrogate were being miscompiled, silently collapsing spike rate and roughly doubling val_ppl with no visible error. One @torch.compiler.disable fixed it; combined with an emulate_precision_casts fix and a separately-compiled MQAR wrapper, whole-model compile is now numerically exact and ~3× faster. reduce-overhead's CUDA Graph mode crashed the live launch on a confirmed open upstream PyTorch bug (grad_accum + checkpoint-recompute); shipped default mode instead. An MQAR-cadence throughput trick was tried and rejected — recall regressed at high N even as general perplexity improved dramatically, the exact failure mode this project's memory-first design watches for. block_size raised 512→640 via a real VRAM probe at true 500M shape, chosen as a genuine quality lever (more context for the memory pathway) not just speed. 500M v3 LAUNCHED — survived one reboot and one VRAM/shared-memory scare, now running steady at ~11.3k tok/s, ~5 days to the 5B-token target.)

46. Three 30M de-risking screens land before 500M v3 finishes; SimPO fails twice at 500M scale despite healthy-looking metrics — reverted to standard DPO

2026-07-20 to 2026-07-27

Three independent 30M screens ran in parallel while 500M v3 trained toward its 5B-token target, each designed to de-risk a post-pretrain pipeline decision before spending 500M-scale GPU time on it.

Sparsity elasticity screen. Refining screen_compile_blk640_30m's pretrain endpoint (spike 0.180, sparsity_lambda: 0.5) at three refine strengths — λ=2.0, 3.0, 5.0 — found λ=3.0 the sweet spot: spike compressed to ~0.099 within ~150M refine tokens before plateauing, val_ppl cost acceptable. λ=2.0 undercompressed; λ=5.0 bought little extra compression for a much larger ppl hit. This screen's ratio — λ=3.0 refine over λ=0.5 pretrain, a 6× multiplier — is what the real 500M sparsity refine was supposed to replicate (see below, where matching the absolute number instead of the ratio cost real compression).

Curriculum warmup A/B: a clean dead heat. Re-run on the current full recipe (curriculum-on vs curriculum-off, otherwise identical), both arms landed at val_ppl 71.48 — not close, identical to two decimal places. Closed; not worth a seed1, not worth revisiting unless the rest of the recipe changes substantially.

LoRA-SFT capacity+budget sweep: plateaus everywhere. Rank 8/16/32 and 100M/300M-token budgets all landed within noise of each other (loss ~4.15–4.25) at 30M scale, while full-finetune SFT on the identical base reached 2.68 — a wide, un-closable gap at this size. Full-FT was adopted for the SFT stage; whether LoRA's gap closes at 500M scale (a much stronger frozen base, proportionally less adaptation needed) was flagged as a separate open question, not answered here.

500M v3 finished at 5.0B tokens, but the sparsity refine that followed underperformed the 30M screen's prediction — a units bug, not an architecture problem. The screen's ratio was λrefine/λpretrain = 6× (3.0/0.5). The real refine config used λ=3.0 too — but 500M v3's own sparsity_lambda had been bumped mid-run from 0.5 to 1.0 (see the previous chapter's spike-creep fix), so the real multiplier was only 3×, not 6×. Compression fell short accordingly: spike 0.295→0.229 (22%) instead of the screen's ~45–54%. Diagnosed after the fact by comparing multipliers, not absolute λ values — the fix (a corrected λ=6.0 pass, properly 6× over the pretrain endpoint's 1.0) is staged as sparsity_cleanup_500m_v3.yaml, queued to run after the alignment stage below.

A WSL auto-upgrade killed the running SFT stage mid-chain with no warning. Recovery was clean: train_sft.py's existing auto-resume (glob latest step_*.pt in the run's own checkpoint dir) picked back up within roughly two minutes of lost progress, verified by checking the resumed step count and token total against the last log line before the kill. Full-FT SFT on the 500M v3 base (sft_500m_full_v3) completed shortly after, 500M tokens.

SimPO failed twice at 500M scale — and the standard preference-optimization metrics did not catch it. SimPO (arXiv:2405.14734, reference-free preference optimization) was chosen for the alignment stage over standard DPO specifically to skip the reference model's ~2× VRAM and compute cost. Two attempts, both real quality regressions invisible to loss/accuracy:
  • v3 (β=2.0, the paper's high-data-regime default): collapsed to incoherent word-salad output by step 9000 of 24000 (37% through its 500M-token budget). Loss saturated near zero and stayed there for the rest of the run — the textbook signature, easy to spot in hindsight.
  • v3b (β=0.4, gentler, hypothesized to avoid v3's saturation): avoided saturation — loss stayed a healthy-looking 0.47 — but degraded anyway, into a progressive repetition lock ("generating electricity generating electricity …" ×12) visible only by actually reading generated text at step 4000 of 7324 (55% through a deliberately-shortened 150M-token budget). Loss and chosen>rejected accuracy gave no warning.

The fix that came out of this: generate real text periodically, and calibrate before concluding damage. gen_check() (in train_sft.py, shared by train_dpo.py) now runs a fixed greedy-decoded prompt every gen_check_every steps and prints it to the log — ~0.8% overhead, and it's what caught v3b's collapse that the metrics missed. Equally important: before trusting gen-check output as evidence of damage, the same prompt was run through the known-undamaged SFT baseline under identical (greedy) decoding first. Without that calibration step, v3b's repetition would have looked identical to ordinary small-model-plus-greedy-decoding repetitiveness — the 30M SFT baseline loops almost as much under greedy decoding as a healthy DPO run does. The calibration check on the 500M checkpoints was decisive precisely because the 500M SFT baseline does not loop under greedy (word-salad, but no lock), which is what made v3b's repetition lock diagnostic rather than ambiguous.

Reverted to standard, reference-anchored DPO (β=0.1) — the KL anchor SimPO was designed to skip turns out to matter at this scale. Currently running on the 500M SFT'd base: ~2,000 tok/s (roughly half SimPO's throughput, the reference model's forward pass is the direct cost), gen-check output matching the SFT baseline's word-salad pattern with no repetition lock so far, chosen>rejected accuracy varying rather than saturating. The corrected sparsity cleanup (λ=6.0, then optionally λ=10.0 as a Pass 2) is staged to auto-launch once this completes.

Major version: 1.30 (Three 30M screens de-risked post-pretrain decisions ahead of 500M-scale spend: sparsity elasticity found λ=3.0/pretrain-λ = 6× as the compression sweet spot, curriculum warmup closed as a dead heat, LoRA-SFT plateaus far short of full-FT at 30M (gap re-tested at 500M later). 500M v3 finished pretrain at 5.0B tokens; its sparsity refine undercompressed because the real config matched the screen's absolute λ rather than its ratio (pretrain λ had been bumped mid-run) — a corrected pass is staged. Full-FT SFT completed cleanly (surviving a mid-chain WSL auto-upgrade kill via existing checkpoint auto-resume). SimPO failed twice as the alignment stage — once with an obvious loss-saturation signature, once with loss/accuracy looking entirely healthy while the model progressively degraded into repetition — establishing that reference-free preference optimization is unsafe at 500M scale here, and that generation quality has to be checked directly, not inferred from preference-loss metrics. gen_check() landed as a permanent in-training safeguard. Alignment stage reverted to standard reference-anchored DPO, currently running.)

47. DPO stopped early on repetition drift; sparsity cleanup Pass 1 wins over Pass 2 — 500M v3 pipeline done, Pass 1 endpoint is the demo artifact

2026-07-28 to 2026-07-29

The standard-DPO stage from §46 ran healthy through step 5500-6000 (~68M tok, val_ppl and gen-check both clean), then progressively drifted into repetition as it cycled the 60k UltraFeedback preference pairs. Gen-check compounded: step 8000 mildly off, step 11000 one clause repeated verbatim, step 12000 multiple overlapping repeated fragments. Both preference-optimization attempts on 500M v3 (two SimPO variants, one standard DPO) now failed — different mechanisms, same outcome: the 500M base is too small for repeated passes over a 60k-pair preference set without overfitting to superficial patterns.

Stopped standard-DPO at step 8000 (~115M tok, not the 300M target) and preserved that checkpoint separately (models/checkpoints/dpo_500m_standard_v3_preserved/step_0008000.pt) as the least-bad snapshot for downstream reuse. Added checkpoint-resume support to train_dpo.py (glob-latest pattern mirroring train_sft.py) — this was already queued in the previous handoff, needed here to keep DPO restartable if we retry with a different budget or strategy later. Smoke-verified on a 30M throwaway before touching the 500M run.

Cross-stage checkpoint resume needed a graceful-fallback patch to train_gpu.py. Seeding the sparsity_cleanup Pass 1 run dir with the DPO step-8000 ckpt and starting train_5090_gpu.py crashed inside optimizer.load_state_dict with “loaded state dict contains a parameter group that doesn't match the size of optimizer's group.” Root cause: DPO used Muon (49 param groups from the muon+adamw split under its config) while Pass 1 uses NorMuon (38 param groups). Outer count matches the naive length check that already existed, but internal group shapes differ. Fixed with a small try/except that catches ValueError/KeyError from the loop and falls back to fresh optimizer state instead of crashing — same graceful path already existed for the length-mismatch case, now covers the shape-mismatch case too. Re-launched Pass 1 cleanly with the patch in place, verified by the [warn] optimizer state shape mismatch (cross-stage resume?); starting fresh log line and by the resumed step counter matching (step 8000, 114.7M tok).

The overnight cleanup chain (Pass 1 λ=6.0 then Pass 2 λ=10.0) ran unattended and completed in ~6h25m — Pass 1 in 3h51m, Pass 2 in 2h33m — both healthy through their token budgets with periodic checkpoint saves as the only chain markers. The 265M-tok Pass 1 endpoint (sparsity_cleanup_500m_v3/step_0016388.pt) and 365M-tok Pass 2 endpoint (sparsity_cleanup2_500m_v3/step_0021969.pt) were both retained for the morning eval sweep.

The eval sweep decided which endpoint is the demo artifact

Split across both GPUs (SFT + DPO on 5090, Pass 1 + Pass 2 on 5080), 4 checkpoints × 3 eval families — ppl_eval, engram_factual_eval, recall_eval --replay split. Completed in ~15 min total.

Ckptval_pplspikemem-Δrecall N=4N=16N=64
sft_500m_full_v310590.283+17,26695%53%41%
dpo_500m_standard_v3_preserved12720.277+35,64994%59%41%
sparsity_cleanup_500m_v3 (Pass 1)43.30.110+29889%47%22%
sparsity_cleanup2_500m_v3 (Pass 2)42.80.075+26364%28%12%
Pass 1 is the final artifact. Hit all success criteria (spike ≤0.15 ✓, memory-Δ >+250 ✓, val_ppl massively improved ✓, recall preserved to ~half of SFT ✓). Pass 2 traded ~40-50% relative recall (N=64 dropped 22%→12%, essentially near chance) for a marginal spike improvement (0.110→0.075) and near-zero val_ppl gain (43.3→42.8) — overcompressed. The val_ppl collapse from ~1200 down to 43 is because both cleanup passes trained on pretraining data — they pulled the model back onto the pretrain distribution the SFT/DPO stages had drifted from, alongside the sparsity work. DPO's memory-Δ being 2× SFT's (+35,649 vs +17,266) is a memorization signature, consistent with the overfit that stopped the run; DPO gave nothing on recall (within noise of SFT) so was net-negative on this pipeline.

Fourth and fifth instances of the build()-drift bug class landed this session and were fixed the same way as the prior three. scripts/chat.py and scripts/recall_eval.py both had local build() functions with kwarg lists that predated engram_v2/mtp/gdn2_chunked/z_loss_weight and were silently instantiating partial 500M models (chat.py reported 316M params vs the real 479M — ~163M dropped as unexpected state_dict keys on load, leaving critical heads randomly initialized). Chat.py's degenerate output (“periodic periodic periodic…”, “M"M"M"…”, dashes) that first read as ckpt-quality damage was actually a broken loader — delegating to train_gpu.build_model restored the SFT ckpt to correctly answer “The capital city of France is Paris.” Same one-line delegation fix applied to recall_eval before the eval sweep, precisely so the sweep numbers would be trustworthy. Reported param count against the training log is the reliable drift signature — anything below 479M for these ckpts means the loader isn't building the right model.

48. The demo model can't chat — a 3-cause coherence diagnosis, a redesign, and a seed variance that blocks scaling

2026-07-31 to 2026-08-01

We set out to scope a 1B model for a community/investor demo and instead discovered the 500M v3 “demo artifact” from §47 can't hold a conversation. A 13-prompt REPL test on the Pass-1 endpoint returned near-pure word salad; the SFT checkpoint (sft_500m_full_v3) was better but still 1/13 clearly right. The Pass-1 verdict had been made on offline metrics (val_ppl 43.3, recall, spike) — chat quality was never in the success criteria. We split the “final artifact” designation accordingly (offline-metrics: Pass 1; chat-demo: SFT) and went looking for why chat is so weak.

Three root causes, all confirmed

The diagnosis (full detail in docs/model_quality_redesign.md and memory neuro_coherence_diagnosis_2026_07_31): (1) catastrophic undertraining — 5B pretrain tokens = 10 tok/param, when SmolLM-360M (a smaller model) used 600B, ~120× more; 29B tokens were already tokenized on disk and unused. (2) Zero attention layers — every token reaches the past only through the fixed-size Hebbian matrix; every competitive brain-inspired model (MiniMax, Jamba, SpikingBrain) interleaves attention, SpikingBrain putting full attention every 7th layer. (3) A lossy 16k vocab that silently drops ~10% of tokens on encode — and it's content words that vanish (“nephew”, “ATP”, “mitochondria” all → deleted; science text only 75% survival, measured in temp/diag_vocab.py). That last one mechanistically explains the vague-filler failure mode: the model was literally trained on “The ___ is the ___ of the cell.” Crucially, the novel core is not the problem — ablating the Hebbian memory sends val_ppl 40.5→373 (memory-Δ +332), it's carrying most of the model's predictive power.

The redesign, screened at 30M and 90M

Built src/hybrid_attn.py (HybridAttentionBlock: full softmax MHA + RoPE + SwiGLU) as a drop-in matching SpikingHebbianBlock's forward_sequence interface, wired via an attention_layer_positions config list, verified byte-identical when unset. Replaced the 16k lossy LUT with a top-32k most-frequent Llama-3 subset vocab (~2-3% drop vs the old ~10%, and KD-compatible — a from-scratch 32k BPE was built first, zero-drop, but abandoned because its token boundaries break KD). The redesigned recipe trains cleanly end-to-end at 30M and 90M with healthy β throughout.

KD rejected across three alphas. The proven path for good spiking models is distillation from a strong teacher (SpikingMamba, SpikingBrain, LAS/FAS all convert-or-distill). But three 30M A/Bs against a matched no-KD control (Llama-3.2-1B teacher) all came back worse, gap widening within each run: alpha=0.5 was +8→+18→+24% worse, alpha=0.2 was +1→+4→+5% worse. That's the “hard-CE is just better here” signature, not the “KD needs more tokens to pay off” one. Off the table for now. Sparsity elasticity mapped in the same sweep: λ=0.5 brings spike to ~0.155 (the target) with memory-Δ holding at +209; λ=0.7 buys almost nothing further for a real ppl cost.

The finding that stopped everything: a seed variance localized to attention

A routine seed1 reconfirm (the standing 3-strikes rule: MoE, Engram-v1, uniform-recurrent-depth all reversed on reseed) found a large divergence at 30M — and a full night of multi-seed work across both GPUs pinned it down. At 30M, seed0 reaches val_ppl 142 while seed1 and seed2 land at 268/269, tracking each other within 1% at every eval point: seed0 is the lucky outlier, not seed1. Confirmed real, not a GPU/batch artifact, via a same-5090 same-batch re-run matching to within 0.6%. A full 2×2 (seed × attention) ablation localized it: without attention, the two seeds are seed-robust (both 449/328/287 across the whole trajectory); adding attention is the sole source of the variance — it hands seed0 a ~2× improvement (287→142) that seed1/seed2 barely capture (287→268). At 90M the effect shrinks but does not vanish within the token budget (final gap seed1/seed2 ~62% worse than seed0, down from 30M's ~89%, and still decelerating rather than closed). This matches published findings exactly: attention-logit growth is a known instability, and middle-layer attention (ours sits mid-stack) is the least seed-stable part of a transformer.

Ruled out the boring explanations. Not β-collapse — β telemetry healthy in every seed at every scale (0% dead, 0% at_floor). Not trivial component-collapse — temp/diag_attention_utilization.py loaded the seed0 and seed2 90M checkpoints and measured the attention layer's output magnitude: attn/residual norm ratio 1.03 vs 1.11, o_proj |mean| 0.040 vs 0.041, essentially identical. Attention is active in every seed; it just converges to a more-useful function only on the lucky one. Two live hypotheses, both addressed by the same next step: attention-training instability (no QK-norm → unbounded logits; NorMuon's Newton-Schulz on the attention matrices where AdamW is reported more seed-stable), or attention simply undertrained at <1.5 tok/param and stabilizing at real scale.

Not ready to scale — and this is the whole point of finding it now. A 500M/1B run costs GPU-weeks and lands on one seed; with this recipe that seed is a coin-flip between excellent (seed0) and mediocre (seed1/seed2, barely better than no attention). Coherence is a token-budget problem for later; seed-robustness is the blocker for scaling at all. The plan (in docs/model_quality_redesign.md, memory neuro_redesign_seed_variance_2026_08_01): stabilize attention at 30M with 3 seeds — QK-norm first (the standard Qwen3/Gemma3/OLMo free-lunch fix for exactly this instability), then optionally route attention matrices to AdamW and lengthen warmup; win = the 3 seeds converge within noise AND beat the no-attn 287. Then confirm at 90M, do one mid-scale (~200-350M) reality check that the variance is gone not just smaller, and only then scale with the real 100B+ token budget. Multi-seed screening is now standing practice — the 3-strikes rule just became four near-misses.

49. QK-norm fixes seed variance, Phase 1 picks 7:1 on recall, Step 3 diverges — two silent bugs found and fixed, stability restored

2026-08-02 to 2026-08-05

Following §48's plan, we walked the ladder: fix seed variance, screen attention ratio, then the mid-scale reality check. Steps 1 and 2 landed clean wins. Step 3 didn't — but the failure led to two silent bugs and a proper stability fix.

QK-norm confirms at 30M and 90M (Steps 1–2)

Shipped attention_qk_norm in src/hybrid_attn.py (per-head RMSNorm on Q/K before RoPE, canonical order). 30M 3-seed screen collapsed the seed spread 90% → 8% (val_ppl 140.30/149.83/152.06, mean 147.4); 90M 3-seed dropped it 62% → 11% (54.76/52.52/58.53, mean 55.27). H1 (attention instability) confirmed, H2 (undertraining) ruled out — QK-norm alone did it, no fallback stabilizers needed. Details in memory neuro_qknorm_fix_confirmed_2026_08_02.

Phase 1: 7:1 attention wins DECISIVELY on recall — against the literature's direction

Attention-ratio A/B at 90M, target aspect ratio (d=640, n=1536, L=8): 7:1 (attention at [3]) vs 3:1 ([3,6]), 2 seeds each, all on the 5090 to remove GPU confounds. val_ppl was tied (48.56/47.06 vs 47.84/44.78) — but recall (recall_eval --replay split) told a different story: arm A hit 100/95/78/75/59% at N=4/8/16/32/64; arm B collapsed to 31/22/9/6/3%, near chance. Confirmed 2-seed. A control run without --replay split gave the same qualitative answer (arm B broken in-context too), and a follow-up placement ablation ([2,5] instead of [3,6]) was worse: the problem is the COUNT of attention layers, not their position.

The literature's "more attention = more recall" (arXiv 2507.06457) reverses on our recipe. Their metric was in-context recall (RULER); ours (--replay split) is persistent-memory recall — the project's differentiator. On persistent memory, 2 attention layers compete for the MQAR-aug loss without teaching the Hebbian pathway to store keys. Memory-Δ was actually HIGHER in the broken arm (+255 vs +231) — a growing memory-Δ is not proof the pathway learned the right thing; always pair with recall_eval. Standing rule now: don't chase more attention layers based on general recall claims. Details: neuro_phase1_attnratio_finding.

λ=0.5 at the new shape, decisively better on recall too

The overnight λ=0.5-at-new-shape verify (d=640, n=1536, L=8, 90M, 2 seeds) came back a clean win over λ=0.3: val_ppl 44.78/45.51 vs 48.56/47.06, memory-Δ matched, spike rate at target (0.160/0.187 vs 0.278/0.283 — the sparsity constraint actually working), and recall better at the hardest N (100/100/92/72/69% and 100/94/92/88/73% vs 100/95/78/75/59%). λ=0.5 became the settled recipe.

Step 3 (276M, d=1152, n=2752, L=8, arm A, λ=0.5, lr=5e-4) diverged

val_ppl bottomed at 97.93 at step 7000, then climbed monotonically to 115.28 by step 17000. spike rate CLIMBED 0.325 → 0.369 against the sparsity penalty — the exact opposite of the 90M runs where it fell. [beta] telemetry showed blocks 1–2 (feeding the attention at [3]) collapsing to floor (at_floor 0% → 72%/75%, accelerating), blocks 4–7 pinning high (μ 0.90–0.96). Killed at step 17000. Diagnostic arms tested λ and lr in isolation and combined:

armλlrbottom val_pplblk1 at_floor (step ~9500)verdict
original0.55e-497.9372%diverged → 115
λ=1.01.05e-495.5050%slower collapse
lr=3.5e-40.53.5e-461.7864%much better ppl, still collapsed
combined (arm 3)1.03.5e-461.9447%best, but same failure mode

Hyperparameters only delayed the collapse. A diag_gate_saturation2.py forensic on arm-3 checkpoints found gate-specific weight blowup: W_fdecay grew 3.09× in blk1 and 3.92× in blk2 vs 1.36–1.68× elsewhere, while ordinary matrices grew uniformly ~2×. A follow-up diag_gate_distributions.py hooking all three GDN-2 gates on arm 3 revealed what the [beta] line had been hiding: blk0's decay-logit std at 7.34 (healthy blocks 2.2–2.9), blks 4–7 erase gates 58–92% saturated. Two of three gates had been unmonitored this entire investigation.

Root cause: Muon runs with ZERO weight decay, and structurally CANNOT have any

The Muon class had no weight_decay parameter at all. build_muon_adamw passed the config's weight_decay only into AdamW, and no active config set it either. So W_k, W_v, W_q, W_ff, to_current, W_w, W_b, and every attention matrix trained with zero weight decay and no way to enable it. Moonshot's Muon is Scalable for LLM Training names this exact failure: “Two crucial techniques for scaling up Muon are (1) adding weight decay and (2) carefully adjusting the per-parameter update scale.” “Vanilla Muon initially converges faster, but model weights grew too large over time, potentially limiting the model's long-term performance.” We had technique #2 (muon.py's sqrt(max/min shape) update scale); we did not have #1. Causal chain: no wd → weights grow unbounded (measured 2.0–2.8× over 12k steps) → gate logits W_gate(spk) grow with them → sigmoid saturates → gradient dies → gates freeze → blocks 1–2 lose their memory → divergence. It bit at 276M but not 107M because more active spike units cross the saturation threshold sooner.

Second bug found in the same audit: energy report vs a 2-LAYER baseline

Analyzing the accumulated energy data across runs, the ratio looked like it dropped below 1.0 at the n/d=2.4 aspect ratio (30M/90M runs "won" at 1.17×/1.12×, then 104M/276M "lost" at 0.88×/0.82×). One step from reporting the aspect ratio had destroyed the efficiency claim. It was a config-key bug: train_gpu.py:822 read c.get("n_layer", 2) but spiking configs write n_layers (plural) — the lookup ALWAYS missed. Every energy report this project has ever printed benchmarked our 4–8 layer spiking model against a 2-layer transformer. Fixed to c.get("n_layer", c.get("n_layers", 2)). Recomputed at matched depth: 30M 1.47×, 90M 1.90×, 104M 1.79×, 276M 1.99× at seq 512, 2.7–2.9× at seq 4096. The efficiency claim is ~2× stronger than we've been reporting AND improves with scale. All past energy numbers in logs/docs/blogs are understated. A follow-up config-key audit (temp/audit_config_keys.py) found no other silent-key bugs of this class.

Two fixes implemented, both back-compat, one WSL incident, arm B running clean

Landed both fixes with unit tests. Muon weight decay: decoupled p*=(1-lr*wd) using the unscaled group lr; the AdamW group is now split so wd hits only 2D weights — decaying RMSNorm gains, biases, gate_bias_f, or gdn2_a toward zero would sabotage the fix (verified 12-decay/44-nodecay params). Decay exact to 8.6e-9. Gate-norm (gdn2_gate_norm): RMSNorm on all three GDN-2 gate pre-activations plus gate_bias_f preserving W_fdecay's -3.0 slow-decay init. Both default off; the real 276M checkpoint still loads clean 0-missing/0-unexpected. [gates sat] telemetry added — erase and decay saturation are no longer invisible. Details: docs/pre_350m_gotchas.md and neuro_step3_divergence_2026_08_04.

WSL VRAM incident. Two batch-28 arms with gate-norm on the 5090 died at step ~2500 with clean process exits, no Python traceback. dmesg revealed dxgk: dxgkio_make_resident: Ioctl failed: -12 (ENOMEM) ×3 — the WSL GPU-residency layer refusing an allocation. Gate-norm adds activation memory (3 RMSNorms × d_mem × 7 spiking blocks) — batch 28 tipped over dedicated VRAM into WDDM shared spill (throughput 9k → 1.1k tok/s = paging thrash), then a residency call hard-failed as the caching allocator fragmented. The signature is sudden throughput collapse, not gradual creep. Fix: batch 16 / grad_accum 2 (eff 32), which sits at 20.3 GB reserved with 12 GB headroom. Any run with gdn2_gate_norm: true must budget for it — do not inherit batch 28.
Arm B (gn+wd, hot lr 5e-4, batch 16) is now past 88.5M tokens with val_ppl 63.60 and STILL DESCENDING — below arm 3's global bottom of 97.93 by 35%. spike rate 0.151, sitting exactly on the 0.15 target. blk1/blk2 at_floor 1–3% (arm 3 was 47–66% here); decay-logit std ~1.0 across all blocks (arm 3 blew up to 6–7). Cleared the ~36M-token point where the batch-28 arms crashed on VRAM, cleared the ~72M-token danger zone where arm 3 collapsed, and is now well into what used to be the divergence tail with a monotonic descent. The combined fix works.

50. The divergence fix confirmed on recall, gate-norm proven load-bearing, a hot LR repeats the Phase-1 anti-pattern — then a two-win composition produces the best recall this project has ever measured

2026-08-05 to 2026-08-08

Picking up from §49's fix: arm B (step3fix_gnwd_hot_b16, gate-norm + Muon weight decay, batch 16/ga2, hot lr 5e-4) had trained clean past every prior danger zone with val_ppl monotonically descending. The open question was whether that was real memory or a val_ppl mirage — the exact failure mode §49's Phase 1 had already caught once (val_ppl tied, recall collapsed).

Arm B confirms clean: it wasn't a mirage

recall_eval --replay split on arm B's step 8000 checkpoint (131M tok): 100/98/89/66/27% at N=4/8/16/32/64, mean rank 7 at N=4 — better than the 90M Phase-1 baseline at 2.5× the params but less than the token budget. The fix is real, not cosmetic.

P1 recipe screen: one variable each, four arms, 200M tokens each

Rather than commit the full 3-seed 1.1B-token budget to a recipe that had never been screened past the divergence fix itself, four single-variable arms ran off arm B first:

armchange vs arm Bval_pplrecall N=4/8/16/32/64verdict
W1gdn2_gate_norm: falsen/a — watchdog aborted17/17/9/3/0% (chance)gate-norm is REQUIRED, not optional
W2sparsity_lambda: 0.5 (was 1.0)54.27100/95/91/70/34%win — sharper rank quality
W3lr: 7e-4 (Step-Law probe)70.88 (worse)100/98/83/67/33%rejected
W4grad_accum: 4 (eff batch 64)38.95 (best)100/100/95/80/33%win — best of the four
W1 answered the open question from §49 decisively: weight decay alone does not bound gate-logit growth. A new watchdog (dstd > 3.0 for two consecutive evals, added this session) auto-aborted W1 at step 2500 — blk0's decay-logit std climbed 1.0→1.5→3.0→4.5→6.2→7.0 across five evals, with decay-gate saturation rising 9%→70% in lockstep, the exact signature that took until step 7000 to show up in val_ppl during the original divergence. Recall confirms it wasn't a near-miss: 17/17/9/3/0%, indistinguishable from chance. Gate-norm and weight decay are not redundant — both are load-bearing.

W3 repeated §49's Phase-1 anti-pattern almost exactly. Step Law (arXiv 2503.04715) predicts lr≈1.0e-3 is optimal for a 1.1B-token run at this size, so 7e-4 looked like a safe step up from 5e-4. Val_ppl came out worse (70.88 vs 55.30) and memory-Δ was much higher (+4346 vs arm B's ~+800) — but recall rank quality degraded (rank@N=4 = 189 vs arm B's 7). Same shape as the 3:1-attention rejection: a growing memory-Δ with degrading recall is not a quality signal, it's the tell. lr stays at 5e-4.

W4 confirmed the other half of the Step-Law reading from docs/pre_350m_gotchas.md item 3: batch 28 (14,336 tok/step) was roughly 6× smaller than Step Law's own batch-size rule suggests for a 1.1B-token run. Raising effective batch to 64 via grad_accum: 4 won on every axis at once — best val_ppl, best recall at every N, no stability cost.

W5 — composing W2's λ=0.5 with W4's grad_accum=4 — produced the best recall this project has ever measured, at LESS training than arm B's own baseline. recall_eval on step 4000 (131M tok, the same token count as arm B's full eval): 100/100/97/91/41% at N=4/8/16/32/64, mean rank 1 at both N=4 and N=8. Beats arm B (100/98/89/66/27%), W2, and W4 individually, and beats them at fewer tokens than either single-change arm needed. The two wins compose without interference — same class of result as the NorMuon+Engram-v2 combined bake, now repeated for gate-norm-era hyperparameters. This is the flagship recipe base: everything from arm B, plus sparsity_lambda: 0.5 and grad_accum: 4.

An overnight chain (temp/chain_overnight_2026_08_07.sh) automated the handoff: poll W5 for exit, run recall_eval on its checkpoint, gate on thresholds (N=4≥95%, N=16≥80%, N=64≥25%) seeded from arm B's own numbers, and only then launch the 2-seed variance check (p4_seed0/p4_seed1) that the original Step 3 was scoped to answer. The gate passed automatically at 01:15 and both seeds launched without anyone watching.

A SIGINT-timeout bug, caught twice on real runs

The chain bounds each run by wall-clock (timeout --signal=INT -k 60 <seconds>), relying on a SIGINT handler added this session (src/train_gpu.py: a non-raising handler sets a flag, the training loop's own while condition checks it and falls through to the existing final-save code, avoiding a bare KeyboardInterrupt mid-backward()). It worked, but -k 60 turned out to be too short twice in a row. W5's own 8h timeout produced no visible handler output and no fresh checkpoint. P4 seed 0's 14h timeout hit the clearest case: a truncated step_0012351.pt.tmp at 82% written, SIGKILLed mid-save — safe (the atomic tmp-then-rename never completed, so no corrupt checkpoint could ever be loaded), but the last 351 steps were lost. P4 seed 1's first 14h timeout was more informative still: the checkpoint write completed (full-size, cleanly renamed) but the process was SIGKILLed moments later, before it could exit — implicating the trailing end-of-run evaluate() + energy.compare() calls, not the save itself, as what overruns a 60-second grace window. Bumped to -k 300 and manually relaunched seed 1, which resumed cleanly at step 4123 with zero token loss.

Polish pass: a real train/inference vocab mismatch, a NaN guard, code hygiene

With the 5090 running the P1/W5 screen, a parallel code review turned up a genuine bug in the inference-time scripts. vocab_map_32768.npy maps 74.5% of raw Llama-3 ids to student id 0 (the rare-token bucket); in real training data specifically, ~4.0% of tokens land on id 0 (measured across 10M sampled tokens and the val tail). Training (data_stream.py) keeps id 0 in the sequence — but generate.py, lm_eval.py, and chat.py were all silently dropping id 0 on encode, making inference sequences ~4% shorter than what the model was trained on. This directly explains the garbled "the of the" / "of theity" pattern in early generate.py samples — content words like "capital" and "machine" were vanishing without trace. Fixed all three scripts to preserve id 0 on encode and emit a visible <unk> on decode instead of silently dropping; an lm_eval re-run on arm B came back within N=300 noise of the pre-fix numbers (HellaSwag 30.3/24.0%, ARC-Easy 31.3/27.7%, ARC-Challenge 16.7/21.7%), confirming the fix is methodologically correct without being a magic quality lever on its own — the model is still heavily undertrained for benchmark-style eval at this token count.

Also landed: a NaN/Inf guard on the clipped gradient norm (train_gpu.py) that skips optimizer.step() on a non-finite grad and aborts after 3 consecutive hits — found defensively during the review, not triggered live, but a real gap since none of the existing dstd/at_floor/spike watchdogs can catch a NaN (nan > threshold is always False). brain_domain_transfer.py's checkpoint loader was hardcoded to a legacy run dir; parametrized on --run so the plasticity axis is finally measurable on current checkpoints. A config-key audit re-run found kd_teacher_topk as a genuinely dead knob and confirmed MQAR's key/value id ranges are still hardcoded to the old 16k-vocab range — both flagged, not yet fixed, in docs/polish_recommendations_2026_08_07.md alongside the rest of the pre-flagship backlog.

Status: P4's 2-seed variance check is running on the composed recipe. If the spread stays small (target ≤15%, matching the QK-norm-era post-fix spread) and doesn't grow, the next step is the 363M flagship (d=1280/n=3072/L=9) at ~7B tokens — the first Chinchilla-ratio pretrain this project will have run. Full detail: docs/plan_2026_08_05.md, docs/polish_recommendations_2026_08_07.md, memory neuro_composed_recipe_confirmed_2026_08_07.

51. The 2-seed gate passes at a tighter statistical read; a pre-flagship audit finds three real bugs and rewrites the plan's timeline; a chunk-size win on the 5080 fails to transfer to the 5090

2026-08-08

P4's two seeds both finished clean overnight. The recall gate was re-run at --B 256 instead of the default 64 — at B=64 the binomial standard error at N=64 (p≈0.4) is ~6 points, wide enough to muddy exactly the high-N region where the composed recipe's edge over arm B lives. At B=256: seed 0 (393M tok) 100/99/96/85/50%, seed 1 (531M tok) 100/100/98/82/52% at N=4/8/16/32/64 — max spread 3 points at every N, seed 0 holding despite 26% fewer tokens (lost to the -k 60→300 relaunch).

Recipe confirmed. Gate-norm + Muon weight decay + λ=0.5 + grad_accum=4 is seed-robust at 276M and is now the flagship base without qualification.

A pre-flagship code audit, before committing GPU-days

With the gate passed, a full read of the pretrain/eval path (docs/pre_flagship_audit_2026_08_08.md) turned up three real bugs and two measured numbers that revised the plan:

  • W_fdecay routes to the wrong optimizer. src/muon.py's gate-matrix filter matches any parameter name containing "decay" — catching not just the intended 1D/degenerate gates but W_fdecay.weight, a full (d_mem×n_neurons) matrix. GDN-2's three gates end up optimized asymmetrically: erase/write get Muon at muon_lr×muP scale, decay gets AdamW at lr — roughly 15× different step magnitude on the one gate that controls forgetting. Added muon_precise_gate_filter (default off, no existing checkpoint affected) to move exactly W_fdecay.weight when flagged on; whether to flip it is deferred to a screen (see below), not decided by inspection alone.
  • energy.py's efficiency-ratio formula predates both GDN-2 and hybrid attention. It never counted the attention block's FLOPs at all, and under-counted GDN-2's spike-driven projections (3 vs the real 7: k/v/q/ff plus the three gates). Both errors inflated the reported advantage the same direction. Rewritten; the 276M recipe's honest ratio is 1.57× at seq 512 / 2.02× at seq 4096, down from the stale 1.99×/2.74× §49 reported. The old "spiking cost is constant in seq_len" claim is also now false whenever an attention layer is present — noted explicitly in the new output.
  • empirical_energy.py has been unrunnable on any current checkpoint since the 20M era — hardcoded run directories, and a --compile-mode default of reduce-overhead, the one compile mode this project has banned from every recipe since the MTP-silent-drop investigation. Added --run/--tf-run, flipped the compile default.

Two measurements changed the flagship's shape directly: the 363M/276M step-time ratio measured 1.216× (eager, a lower bound), which against P4's live 8.0–8.8k tok/s puts the flagship at ~10 days, not the plan doc's 8.5 — a throughput the 276M model itself doesn't reach with gate-norm on. And a VRAM slope fit (calibrated against P4's own live numbers, which retrodicted the §49 WDDM incident almost exactly) showed block_size: 768 needs ~35.4 GB at batch 16, more than the 5090 has — locked to 512.

Three small fixes, one deferred by design

Landed and unit-tested same session: signed decay-gate telemetry (the old decay_sat conflated never-forget and fast-forget saturation into one number that couldn't tell them apart — split into decay_sat_lo/decay_sat_hi; measured on p4_seed0, 100% of blk0's 35.4% saturation is the never-forget direction, not diverging but previously invisible); restored [alpha] telemetry under GDN-2 (never populated on that code path — the line has been silently dead since GDN-2 landed); and a snapshot_brain RNG leak (the brain-snapshot block drew from np.random without the save/restore guard evaluate() uses, so the "harmless" diagnostic snapshot was quietly perturbing which training windows got sampled).

A spike-aggregation dilution fix was also built (attention-layer zero-stubs dilute the flat-mean spike rate the sparsity penalty consumes by ~11–14%; a spike_rate_undiluted flag plus the retune math to preserve identical training pressure) but — Glenn's explicit call — deferred rather than applied, to avoid invalidating the just-confirmed 2-seed recipe without its own screen.

Two speed knobs: one real, one shelved

The 5080's win didn't transfer. gdn2_chunked_dense is an exact reassociation of the same recurrence at any chunk size; a chunk-size sweep on the idle 5080 showed 256 at 1.46× chunk 64's speed and 18% less peak VRAM, and a dedicated parity test (temp/test_gdn2_chunk_sizes.py) confirmed 128/256/512 are numerically exact against the sequential oracle (forward rel-err 2.2e-6, backward 6.7e-6, including the MQAR-style correlated-keys stress regime). Restarting the just-launched W_fdecay screen (see below) at chunk=256 to validate on real hardware, the 5090 at batch 16 told a different story: 7,200–8,900 tok/s, noisy and still climbing, against chunk 64's clean 8,064 baseline — roughly on par, not a decisive win. The 5080 sweep was launch-bound at small batch; the 5090 at flagship batch size is not, and that regime difference is the whole story. Chunk size stays an open question, to be settled by a clean single-process 5090 probe rather than trusted from the 5080.

The second candidate, caching the LIF _scan_chunked decay matrix (rebuilt up to ~40–70 times per forward at the flagship's depth, all eager since lif_parallel is @torch.compiler.disable), looked safe on paper — the cache key is a complete function of (beta, T, device, dtype) and a single-forward torch.equal check passed. A 12-step real-training determinism check caught what the single-tensor check couldn't: losses diverged starting at step 2 (max abs diff 6e-2), an autograd/reuse-across-steps interaction. Compiled speed was also mixed (1.03× at B=4, 0.69× at B=6). Shelved without further investigation — the lesson generalizes: verify a training-loop change against a multi-step loss trajectory, not one forward pass, especially anywhere near lif_parallel, which has already miscompiled silently once (§44/45).

Status: x1_fdecay_adamw/x1_fdecay_muon (200M tok each, gdn2_chunk_size: 256) running on the 5090 to decide the W_fdecay routing question, chaining to a paired recall_eval --B 256. An X2 hygiene screen (winner + the dilution fix, retuned) was built and queued (temp/gen_x2_hygiene.py, temp/chain_x2_after_x1.sh) but killed per Glenn's decision to defer both the dilution fix and X2 itself for now. A clean single-process 5090 probe (temp/probes_5090_after_x1.sh) is queued behind X1: max-autotune vs default compile mode, and whether the chunk-256 VRAM saving buys back a larger batch or block_size: 768 at the flagship shape. LR stays 5e-4 (Step Law's ~6e-4 not re-screened this session — W3 already rejected 7e-4 on the same recall-rank-vs-memory-Δ anti-pattern). Token budget for the flagship set to 5.5B (~15 tok/param), Glenn's call over the plan's 7B, trading ~1.5 days of runtime for schedule margin on an unattended ~10-day run. Full detail: docs/pre_flagship_audit_2026_08_08.md, memory neuro_pre_flagship_audit_2026_08_08.

52. The flagship diverges and is killed — its best checkpoint becomes a working instruct model, three hypotheses die as nulls, and a matched-transformer baseline plus a disciplined plan replace the module lottery

2026-08-25 to 2026-08-28

The flagship (§51/v1.36) ran healthy for days — val_ppl bottomed 19.61 at step 124k — then reversed and climbed every eval to 24.75 by 134k, at the cosine LR minimum where a runaway shouldn't happen. We killed it at 135k/149k and kept step_0124000.pt (the val-min) as the artifact; a backup already sat on the H: drive.

Root cause: runaway LM-head weight-norm growth. Checkpoint forensics showed head.weight L2 flat at ~150 for 12k steps then going superlinear (+1.4 → +10.5 → +33.6 per 4k steps, +29% total), the MTP heads following, while every block moved less (0.66–0.77×) and the embedding decayed normally. Bigger head → sharper logits → overconfidence → rising ppl. Not LR, not data-wrap, not resume, not numerics — all four eliminated by forensics. z_loss_weight: 1e-4 was too weak to hold it, and nothing in the live log showed it: the dstd/at_floor/spike watchdogs were all blind (max dstd 1.8 vs 3.0; spike 0.152 vs 0.20). Detail: neuro_flagship_divergence_root_cause.

Three guards shipped in response, default-on and cost-free: a [wnorm] telemetry line (head/embed/mtp norms + %-change every eval), a val_ppl divergence tripwire (best-so-far + patience, saves and exits 2 — a unit test on the real flagship sequence trips it at step 130k, 5k steps before we killed by hand), and a z_loss_include_mtp option.

The killed run becomes a working instruct model

We SFT'd the val-min artifact on Alpaca (60M tok, 6.5h). The in-training gen-check looked repetition-locked at every checkpoint — but that was greedy decoding on a bare, non-Alpaca prompt, out-of-distribution for an instruct model. Under the trained ### Instruction: format with sampling (or even greedy):

It works. "What is the capital of France?" → "The capital of France is Paris." + clean EOS, both greedy and sampled. Primary-colors and one-sentence prompts land coherently; longer open-ended prompts ramble — the genuine 363M ceiling, not a defect. sft_flagship_363m/step_0003663.pt is the alignment endpoint. lm_eval: arc_easy 38.4% (+13.4pt over chance), hellaswag/arc_challenge at chance. Detail: neuro_sft_flagship_outcome.

The launch audit caught the sixth instance of the hand-rolled-build() drift bug class: train_sft.build_optimizers passed 3 of 11 args to build_muon_adamw, silently dropping normuon/fix_gate_matrices/precise_gate_filter — routing the 9 one-row gate matrices through Muon's Newton-Schulz, the β-killer from §49. The tell: same 194-param total but a 71/123 → 80/114 split shift and "NorMuon" → "Muon". Fixed by delegation (same pattern as the prior five), plus a regression test with a negative control so instance seven is caught automatically. Detail: neuro_sft_optimizer_drift_fix.

Three hypotheses die as clean nulls

With correct routing and the GPUs free, we ran the post-flagship backlog to ground — all three came back negative, the discipline paying off rather than failing:

  • Decay-gate saturation is NOT the recall bottleneck. The block-0 d=neverforget saturation queued as the top A/B was a symptom, not a cause. An inference-only f_logit clamp fully desaturated every block (99/35/42/16% → 0%) and changed split-replay recall by exactly zero; pushing further toward forgetting monotonically destroyed it. Never-forget is load-bearing; recall here is interference/capacity-limited, not retention-limited. The whole planned A/B was cancelled — and its target parameter W_alpha isn't even the active gate under GDN-2 (W_fdecay is), so it would have been a silent no-op. Detail: neuro_decay_gate_saturation_null.
  • DPO still collapses, even with correct routing. The fix made re-testing worthwhile (the §46/§47 500M failures ran with the β-killing bug). It collapsed anyway — word-soup and hallucinated facts by step 100 (0.03 epochs), standard reference-anchored DPO at 0.5 epochs. No DPO stack in this pipeline; the SFT endpoint stands. Detail: neuro_dpo_retest_2026_08_26_null.
  • Stronger z_loss hurts. z_loss_weight 1e-4→1e-3 + include_mtp came back +3.7 to +7.7 val_ppl worse at every eval, and head norm grew more, not less (the two z-loss terms likely fighting through the shared trunk). Don't default it; the free wins are the telemetry + tripwire. Detail: neuro_zloss_ab_2026_08_26_null.

A matched baseline, an LR-fairness lesson, and a reframed plan

To retest the blog's 20M/700M architecture win at flagship scale we built a matched all-attention baseline (baseline_transformer_363m, 362.49M, all HybridAttentionBlock — the fair modern block, not the 29-line TinyTransformer). Two smoke-caught fixes were needed: an all-attention stack has no bounded-spike protection, so it needs final_norm + depth_scaled_init or the raw residual stream into the head explodes loss to ~28000 (both config-gated, default off; neuro_final_norm_attention_stack).

LR fairness matters. A 4-arm sweep found the transformer's best muon_lr=0.02 — the spiking recipe's 0.005 is ~4× too low for it (val_ppl 118 → 98). A same-LR "matched" baseline would have handicapped the transformer ~17% and manufactured a fake architecture win. Sweep the transformer's LR before any matched comparison. Detail: neuro_transformer_baseline_363m.

The baseline runs at the fair LR (~45k tok/s, ~1.3d). Preliminary, not to be over-read at 9%: the transformer sits ~47 val_ppl vs the flagship's ~39–40 at matched tokens — early evidence our arch still leads, but the verdict waits for completion plus a recall compare.

Finally, the strategy. This project has ~11 module-search nulls (MoE, fracconn, SDM, adaptive-compute, PMA, three refines, DPO×3, decay-gate, z_loss). The improvement plan (docs/improvement_plan_2026_08.md) deliberately turns away from more of that toward: Tier-1 known-good — the flagship trained on 4.79B of 14.5B available tokens (33%, ~13 tok/param, below Chinchilla), so flagship-v2 = the confirmed recipe + more tokens + the designed-but-unused context curriculum + a fair/transferable LR (weight-decay-over-μP, arXiv:2510.19093), near-certain gain at zero research risk; and one Tier-2 bet aimed squarely at the proven recall ceiling — log-linear attention (arXiv:2506.04761, composes with GDN-2), scoped (docs/loglinear_attention_design.md) behind a two-stage gate: a kernel de-risking spike before any flagship commit, because the O(log n) hierarchy OOMs at B=18 (~+8.5 GB) and its multiplied decay factors are exactly the overflow class _gdn2_dense_chunk already had to hand-factorize around.

53. Log-linear Stage-A closes on GPU; WSD ships and flagship_v2 diverges at 6.6% of budget — the §52 tripwire fires clean, cosine reroll takes the schedule spot

2026-08-28 to 2026-08-30

Two parallel threads — closing the log-linear kernel risk on the 5080 and shipping flagship-v2 on the 5090 with the WSD scheduler wired in — one ended clean, the other ended with the guard we shipped in §52 firing exactly as designed.

Log-linear Stage-A on GPU

Built src/loglinear.py: an O(T log T) sequential oracle plus a chunkwise-parallel gdn2_loglinear_chunked kernel whose intra-chunk WY math is a copy, not a mutation, of the parity-tested _gdn2_dense_chunk. Wired behind gdn2_loglinear: true in SpikingHebbianBlock + train_gpu.build_model; the tiny W_lw = Linear(n_neurons, L+1) read-weight head init'd near zero so 2·sigmoid(0)=1 reduces to plain GDN-2 at init.

Parity checked on CPU and on the 5080: forward + backward + final-state, reduce-to-GDN-2 (vs gdn2_chunked_dense), and kernel-vs-oracle at general per-level weights — all green at trained-regime α down to 1e-6 under the house max-normalized grad metric (rel err <5e-3). A 500-iter NaN stress at the 30M shape with MQAR-like correlated keys, high erase gates, and 1/3 dead-decay channels produced zero NaN, worst |out|=190, worst |state|=0.66. The design doc's headline fear (multiplied decay factors overflowing the way _gdn2_dense_chunk had already been hand-factorized around) was retired empirically by a shared-reference tower: age the whole tower by each chunk's Gamma_C on the same per-channel clock as the single state, so every block is bounded exactly like the proven single S.

VRAM at the flagship shape measured at ~146 MiB/sample — extrapolated to 8 delta layers × B=18 that is ~+16 GB, which OOMs the 32 GB card. Throughput regression only +4–14%, well under the 25% budget. The naive full-flagship port isn't viable; Stage-B integration will need log-linear on a subset of delta layers or a detach-on-Fenwick-write. A 55M model smoke on the 5080 confirmed the wire (W_lw registers on both delta blocks, grads finite every step, loss 10.19 → 9.94), and a treatment-only 30M screen (configs/screen_loglinear_30m.yaml) ran the full 100M tokens in 3.04h — zero NaN, val_ppl 69.35 at step 6104, memory-Δ +157.98. That closes Stage-A gate (ii) empirically. It is NOT a full Stage-B A/B: the matched control (screen_bake_full_gdn2_30m.yaml) was not run alongside, so no head-to-head win claim yet. Detail: neuro_loglinear_stageA_kernel_2026_08_28.

WSD ships in flagship_v2 — and immediately fails

Per §52's Tier-1 plan, we wired a WSD (warmup→stable→decay) branch alongside the existing cosine lr_scale(). Cosine is byte-preserving on every existing config (22/22 unit checks including a regression assert vs the pre-edit formula). Flagship_v2 (configs/flagship_v2_363m.yaml) launched at 10B tokens with WSD stable phase ending at step 231,391 (~8.53B tok) then linear decay to lr_min_frac=0.1. First step-1000 landed nominal: loss 13.32 / 6,036 tok/s / vram 27.3/32 GB. Then, at step 18k / 271k (6.6% of budget):

step 12000  val_ppl 39.03  ← best
step 14000  val_ppl 39.15  (+0.3%)  DIVERGING x1
step 16000  val_ppl 39.95  (+2.4%)  DIVERGING x1
step 18000  val_ppl 40.41  (+3.5%)  DIVERGING x2
[WATCHDOG] TRIPPED: exceeded best for 2 consecutive evals — saving ckpt, exit 2
saved step_0018000.pt   (best_ckpt = step_0012000.pt)
The §52 val_ppl tripwire fired exactly as designed. Saved the best checkpoint (step_0012000.pt, val_ppl 39.03 at 444M tok), then exited clean with code 2. The forensics investment from the flagship post-mortem paid for itself on the very next long run — roughly two weeks of futile compute avoided. Best-ckpt tracking, [wnorm] telemetry, and the tripwire each played their role.
WSD is unsafe with this recipe. [wnorm] head grew 107.5→126.8→145.6→…→182.9 = +70% over 16k steps. The §52 divergence trace at its worst: +29% over 8k steps. Same mechanism (runaway LM-head weight-norm), ~1.2× faster per step. Cause: WSD keeps LR at peak the entire stable phase; cosine's continuously-decaying LR was silently damping that pressure in mid-training. That protective work was invisible until we removed it. The "quality is a wash at a fixed budget" heuristic I anchored on when scoping WSD is wrong for any recipe with head-norm sensitivity; this one has it, documented since §52. Detail: neuro_flagship_v2_wsd_diverged_2026_08_30.

Cosine reroll

Rolled back by minimum diff: configs/flagship_v2_cosine_363m.yaml is a fork of the v1 flagship (flagship_363m.yaml) with exactly two key changes — run_name and max_tokens (5.5B → 10B). Everything else (the composed recipe, muon_lr=0.005, block_size=512, all guards) is byte-identical to v1; audit confirmed. The WSD attempt's checkpoints stay in models/checkpoints/flagship_v2_363m/ as forensic record; the cosine reroll's ckpts land in a fresh directory. Launched on the 5090, currently at step 1000, loss 13.23, 8,464 tok/s, vram 27.3/32 GB — healthy, ETA ~15–16 days.

Not blocking on log-linear for v2: the plan (§52) always sequenced Tier-2 as a parallel screen feeding a later v3, and the Stage-B A/B (matched control + recall_eval --replay split at N=32/64) remains unrun.

Major version: 1.38 (Log-linear Stage-A CLOSED on GPU.
