54. Log-linear closes negative; the transformer head-to-head gets its missing axes; and the recall ceiling turns out to be a training-recipe bug

2026-08-31 to 2026-09-06

Three threads while flagship_v2_cosine cooked on the 5090: closing log-linear's Stage-B gate, finally measuring the axes the transformer comparison had left blank, and — from what those measurements showed — chasing the associative-recall ceiling to a root cause that turned out to be one config line.

Log-linear Stage-B: CLOSED-NEGATIVE

Ran the real 2×2 A/B (treatment/control × seed 0/1) with an explicit matched control, verified three ways: config diff, a CPU pre-flight asserting the W_lw head is present iff gdn2_loglinear, and the optimizer-routing delta (17/30 vs 15/28 = exactly the 2 W_lw heads, nothing else). The pre-registered win did not just fail — it reversed: plain GDN-2 beat log-linear at N=32 AND N=64 in 6/6 cells (3 eval modes × 2 seeds), val_ppl within noise, memory-Δ higher for the control in 3/4. The reweighted read appears to dilute the clean single-state readout. Do not advance.

One methodological catch worth more than the result: the design doc had pre-registered --replay split as the metric, but tracing the state-carry showed split is structurally ~blind to this treatment — the kernel returns only the collapsed single state (model.py:682 / loglinear.py:273), so split's 1-token second pass reads an empty Fenwick tower and the age-reweighting cannot fire. Scored on split alone we'd have recorded a false "no difference." Rule going forward: screen READ-changing variants single-presentation, not split. Detail: neuro_loglinear_stageA_kernel_2026_08_28.

The transformer head-to-head gets its missing axes

§52 left "lm_eval/recall on both bases" explicitly unrun, so the "our arch wins" claim rested on val_ppl alone. Ran both. Downstream MC: the transformer is competitive-to-better despite its worse perplexity — arc_easy 44.6 vs 38.9, hellaswag 31.4 vs 29.8, both at chance on arc_challenge/mmlu. Neither gap is statistically strong (≈1.9σ and ≈1.3σ) and the transformer had 13% more tokens, so the honest read is "tie-to-slightly-behind," not a rout. In-context recall: a crossover. Spiking wins N≤16 (100/98/94 vs 81/73/58) but collapses at N≥32 (8/6/2%) where the transformer degrades gracefully (55/42/31%); split-replay recovers spiking to 56/27/16.

The small-N recall win is confounded; the transformer's high-N win is not. The spiking model trained WITH MQAR augmentation (mqar_aug=True, N=16); the transformer without. So our small-N lead is partly trained-task familiarity. The transformer beating an MQAR-trained model at N≥32 zero-shot is the robust signal. Net: the win holds on val_ppl (~34%) + the energy / persistent-portable-state / constant-seq-cost differentiators, NOT on downstream MC or high-N recall. Detail: neuro_transformer_baseline_363m.

The recall ceiling: interference, not capacity — and a curriculum bug

Diagnostic first: GDN-2 already runs at n_heads=1, i.e. the full dense d_mem×d_mem state. A megabyte of state cannot literally be too small for 32 bindings, so the ceiling is retrieval interference, not state size — consistent with the 4×-budget null (§-earlier neuro_500m_post_pretrain_refine_null) and the decay-gate null. That kills "just make the matrix bigger" and points at the write/read rule.

Then the cheap lever. The flagship trains MQAR at exactly N=16 (mqar_N_min = mqar_N_max = 16) and collapses at N=32 — the first N it never saw. Literature agrees (arXiv:2507.02782: recurrent models fail to length-generalize when training covers a narrow slice of attainable states; standard MQAR protocol trains N∈[4,64]). A four-point N_max curve at 30M {16,32,48,64} showed both halves of the story: the recall horizon tracks the trained range, but peak recall is maximized at [4,32] and degrades wider (val_ppl improves monotonically as you widen — a real recall-vs-ppl tradeoff). The naive fix (widen to 64) made recall worse at every N. A second knob, MQAR loss weight, looked like a rare double-win at one seed (0.5→1.0: N32 52→70, val_ppl 73.0→68.9) with 2.0 overshooting.

A 2-seed confirm split the recommendation — and that is the point of running two seeds. Baseline (fixed-16 + w0.5) vs proposed ([4,32] + w1.0), 2 seeds each: the recall win is robust (proposed beats baseline at every N in both seeds — N32 70/53 vs 34/33), but val_ppl is seed-fragile for the weight bump (68.92 / 83.41 vs a tight baseline 73.4/73.2; seed 1 descended monotonically 145→83, slow convergence not divergence). Attribution: the RANGE change carries ~2/3 of the recall gain at zero ppl cost; the WEIGHT change adds the rest but owns the variance. Flagship-v3: adopt MQAR range [4,32]; HOLD weight 1.0 pending a 90M/more-tokens check. Detail: neuro_mqar_range_30m_2026_09_03.

Ranked plan for actually raising (not relocating) the ceiling written up in docs/recall_ceiling_design_2026_09.md: free levers (MQAR range, split-replay as default) → cheap code (StateX post-training state expansion, arXiv:2509.22630) → the big bet, DeltaProduct (arXiv:2502.10297), which takes multiple delta steps per token for a diagonal-plus-rank-nh transition — directly more expressive at separating interfering bindings, keeps the fixed-size portable state, and ships a reference kernel ~20% faster than DeltaNet's. Gated behind a de-risking spike, per the log-linear lesson.

Interlude: WSL ate the flagship

WSL auto-upgraded at ~03:58 and killed every process, including the 9-day flagship. Recovered by re-launch — resumed clean from step_0104000 (3.83B tok), ~0 progress lost, and by step 107k it was back at 8,814 tok/s. Two standing actions: launch long runs detached from a real terminal (not a harness-session task), and disable WSL auto-upgrade. Also learned the hard way: don't run screens during the flagship's post-resume recompile — CPU contention roughly doubled it. Detail: reference_wsl_auto_upgrade_kill.

flagship_v2_cosine itself is the good news of the period: through the WSL interruption it has held a clean descent to val_ppl 23.30 at step 122k/271k (45%), memory-Δ ~+340, β 0% dead everywhere, and — the signal that matters — [wnorm] head peaked near step 30k and has been receding ever since (−0.67%/eval). The runaway-head-norm mechanism that killed v1 and the WSD reroll is, for the first time, demonstrably damped.

Major version: 1.39 (Log-linear CLOSED-NEGATIVE at Stage B: real 2×2 with an explicit matched control (verified 3 ways incl. optimizer-routing delta = exactly the 2 W_lw heads) — plain GDN-2 BEAT log-linear at N=32 and N=64 in 6/6 cells, val_ppl within noise; do not advance. Methodological catch: the pre-registered --replay split metric is structurally ~blind to a READ-changing variant (kernel returns only the collapsed single state, so pass-2 reads an empty Fenwick tower) — screen READ-changing variants single-presentation. Transformer head-to-head completed on the axes §52 left blank: transformer competitive-to-BETTER downstream (arc_easy 44.6 vs 38.9, hellaswag 31.4 vs 29.8, both ≈1.9σ/1.3σ and it had 13% more tokens) despite worse ppl, and clearly better high-N in-context recall (spiking single-pres COLLAPSES N≥32 at 8/6/2% vs 55/42/31%; split-replay recovers to 56/27/16). Spiking's small-N recall win is CONFOUNDED (it trained with MQAR aug, the transformer did not); the transformer's zero-shot high-N win is the robust signal. Recall-ceiling diagnosis: n_heads=1 already = full dense state, so the ceiling is INTERFERENCE not size — kills "bigger matrix," points at the write/read rule. Root cause of the N≥32 collapse found: flagship trains MQAR at exactly N=16 (mqar_N_min=mqar_N_max=16). 30M N_max curve {16,32,48,64}: recall horizon tracks the trained range but peaks at [4,32] and DEGRADES wider (naive widening to 64 made recall worse at every N; val_ppl improves monotonically = real recall-vs-ppl tradeoff). MQAR-weight knob 0.5→1.0 looked like a double win at 1 seed (N32 52→70, ppl 73.0→68.9), 2.0 overshoots. 2-seed confirm SPLIT the recommendation: recall win robust (proposed beats baseline every N both seeds, N32 70/53 vs 34/33) but val_ppl seed-fragile for the weight bump (68.92/83.41 vs tight baseline 73.4/73.2; s1 = slow convergence 145→83, not divergence). FLAGSHIP-v3: adopt MQAR range [4,32], HOLD weight 1.0 pending 90M validation. Ceiling-raiser plan in docs/recall_ceiling_design_2026_09.md (free → StateX → DeltaProduct arXiv:2502.10297, gated behind a kernel spike). WSL auto-upgraded and killed all processes incl. the flagship; recovered clean from step_0104000 with ~0 loss. flagship_v2_cosine healthy at val_ppl 23.30 / step 122k (45%), head-norm RECEDING since ~step 30k — the v1/WSD divergence mechanism is damped for the first time.)

55. The range lever confirmed and split-replay banked; three recall levers screened negative; a head-norm stability lever built — then a self-inflicted WSL outage, root-caused and fixed

2026-09-07 to 2026-09-08

A window of 5080-side screening while flagship_v2_cosine kept descending on the 5090 — turning §54's recall findings into a v3 config and testing the obvious next levers — then a Windows-side detour that cost more than any of the science: an auto-update-prevention step I recommended wedged WSL itself.

The range lever confirmed; split-replay is the free high-N answer

§54 left the range recommendation at a 2-seed read. A third seed of the range-only arm ([4,32] + w0.5) reproduced the win with zero seed-overlap: the worst range seed beats the best fixed-16 baseline at every N (N32 52/73/58 vs 34/33, N64 25/17/20 vs 8/9), val_ppl neutral-to-better (72.98/60.28/72.80 vs 73.38/73.24). Attribution settled cleanly — seed 1 is the good seed for range-only (60.28) but the bad seed for range+weight1.0 (83.41 in §54), so the ppl variance is owned by the weight bump, not the range. Then split-replay, quantified on these same checkpoints, turned out to be the real lever for the one axis we lose to the transformer: 2-pass build-then-query takes N=64 from ~17–25% single-pass to 62–77%, and gives usable recall (50–61%) at N=128 where single-pass is dead. The levers compose — range wins the single-pass task, split-replay lifts everyone.

flagship_v3 drafted. configs/flagship_v3_363m.yaml = v2 + one surgical change: mqar_N_min 16→4, mqar_N_max 16→32, hold mqar_weight 0.5. DRAFT only — v2 is ~55% done, so there is a week to fold in any further confirmed lever. Adopt --replay split as the default recall-serving path. Detail: neuro_mqar_range_30m_2026_09_03.

Three levers screened negative — cheaply, before any flagship commit

All one-variable off the confirmed range recipe, all killed at 30M. pre_conv (a causal depthwise conv before k/v/q, cited as a recall help in arXiv:2508.19029) did the opposite: it produced the best val_ppl of any 30M screen (55.5, tight across 3 seeds) while destroying recall to near-chance even at N=4 — a clean ppl/recall decoupling. The conv blurs the exact key token across its window, and exact associative recall needs an exact pointer: a better language model, a broken recall machine. local_attn (BASED-style short sliding window, arXiv:2509.24552) was a null — no recall gain over the pure range recipe, higher seed variance, and it re-adds attention (off the pure-memory moat). muon_lr tested the paper's own hint that recurrent recall is LR-sensitive: it is (N16 recall runs 95→62→53→12 across 0.005/0.01/0.02/0.04), but the recall optimum sits at or below the current 0.005 while val_ppl is minimized at 0.02 — a recall-vs-ppl tradeoff pointing the wrong way for a free lever. Net: the flagship's muon_lr=0.005 is already at/near the recall optimum. Details: neuro_preconv_localattn_null_2026_09_07, neuro_muon_lr_recall_tradeoff_2026_09_07.

A head-norm stability lever, built and gated

The failure mode that killed v1 and the WSD reroll (§52/§53) is runaway LM-head norm; z_loss was the intended guard and 1e-3 was a null. Reading the optimizer routing showed why: the LM head already sits in AdamW's decay group, but AdamW's decoupled decay at the global weight_decay (~lr·wd/step) is far too weak to hold it, and mtp_proj is on the Muon side entirely. Built head_weight_decay (a config-gated dedicated higher-WD AdamW group for the LM head only) into muon.build_muon_adamw + train_gpu._build_optimizers, verified byte-identical when off (the live flagship resumes safely) and correctly isolating head.weight when on. Staged four 30M screens; 30M only tests safety/neutrality since it cannot reproduce a 120k-step divergence — the real test is folding a safe winner into v3.

Interlude, worse than §54's: I wedged WSL

Recommending the unsupported AutoUpgradeViaMsix=0 registry key (to stop WSL auto-updates from killing runs) backfired into a servicing outage. It blocked an in-flight WSL upgrade mid-completion, wedging every wsl.exe call into "WSL is finishing an upgrade... Catastrophic failure / Wsl/CallMsi/Install/E_UNEXPECTED" — surviving multiple reboots, DISM, and a plain AppX reinstall. Root-caused by read-only inspection rather than more blind command-throwing: the Store AppX was healthy and wsl.msi reconfigured successfully on every call (event-log status 0), but a separate leftover MSI product ({104E16F9…}) survived the AppX reinstall and the inbox launcher (10.0.26100.8737) looped trying to reconcile them. There is no user-facing "pending upgrade" flag — the flag is the half-present state. Definitive fix: uninstall the MSI product, remove the AppX for all users, reboot with WSL fully absent (the step never done), let Windows reinstall it clean on boot. The 482 GB Ubuntu-G distro on G: was never at risk (separate VHDX, HKCU-registered; wsl --import-in-place the no-copy safety net). Lesson banked: the risk asymmetry was backwards — a supported setting's worst case is a recoverable killed run; the unsupported key's worst case is an unlaunchable WSL. Detail: reference_wsl_auto_upgrade_kill (RESOLVED 2026-09-08).

flagship_v2_cosine came through both interruptions intact, resuming clean from step_0148000 (5.46B tok, val_ppl 21.59 and still descending, head-norm still receding) — the checkpoint-auto-resume safety net doing exactly its job while the Windows layer burned.

Major version: 1.40 (Range lever 3-seed CONFIRMED, zero seed-overlap — worst range seed beats best baseline at every N (N32 52/73/58 vs 34/33); val_ppl neutral-to-better. Split-replay quantified as the free high-N fix (N64 ~20→~70%, usable N128 50–61%); levers compose. flagship_v3 DRAFTED — configs/flagship_v3_363m.yaml = v2 + range [4,32], hold w0.5; adopt --replay split as the default recall path. 56. flagship_v2 diverged and was salvaged; head_wd confirmed at 363M; DeltaProduct built but dominated by width — the recall ceiling is capacity, not cleverness

2026-09-08 to 2026-09-10

flagship_v2_cosine diverged at step ~170k (watchdog tripped, val_ppl 19.30@162k → 21.36). Post-mortem on the telemetry: mtp_proj norm ran away +34% starting ~4k steps BEFORE the LM head inflected — the MTP projections LED the runaway and dragged the head; gates were innocent (β dead 0%, memory-Δ stable). The earlier §55 note ("mtp_proj is on the Muon side, untouched by head_weight_decay") was exactly the gap. DeltaProduct BUILT with NO new kernel (arXiv:2502.10297, the recall-ceiling "big bet"). The de-risk that gated it: n_h Householder products/token equals running the existing gdn2_chunked_dense recurrence over a sequence expanded T→T·n_h and reading every n_h-th state — verified numerically to 3.5e-15, n_h=1 reduces to DeltaNet exactly, chunked==sequential plumbing, n_h=1 model byte-identical. Cost n_h=2 = +19% params, only ~20% slower (not 2x). Config-gated deltaproduct_nh, bulk in src/deltaproduct.py. Detail: neuro_deltaproduct_built_2026_09_09.
…but DeltaProduct is dominated by simply adding the same params as WIDTH. The param-matched control (n_neurons 1024→1664, +19% params = the SAME budget as DP nh=2, but as width not Householder steps) BEAT DeltaProduct on BOTH recall and val_ppl: single-pres N16/32/64 98/41/19 (ctrlwide) vs 89/34/11 (DP nh=2) vs 80/22/9 (ctrl); split 99/69/39 vs 97/60/32; val_ppl 56.33 vs 59.32 vs 71.85. So DP's apparent win was CAPACITY, not the Householder structure. (CONFIRMED negative 2026-09-10: seed-1 reproduced (nh2 s1 N32=32) and nh=3 did NOT help (N32 dropped to 24) — dominated by plain width at every nh.)

The real signal: the recall ceiling responds to capacity, not to structural cleverness

Read together with §55's negatives (pre_conv, local_attn, loglinear, muon_lr) this is now a pattern: every structural recall mechanism we've tried is null-or-dominated, while a plain +19% width jumped single-pres N32 recall 22→41%. The lever the recall ceiling actually responds to is state/representation CAPACITY. That is exactly what the recall_ceiling_design_2026_09.md ladder's next rung — StateX / state-expansion (Sparse Delta Memory, arXiv:2607.07386) — targets. (Terminology correction: “StateX” = arXiv:2509.22630, a POST-TRAINING state expansion flagged as maybe-under-delivering for our interference-limited delta; the arXiv:2607.07386 Sparse Delta Memory is lever F, DEFERRED over a KV-cache/moat risk — the two are not the same and neither is greenlit-to-build.) The clean next step is to disentangle whether ctrlwide's win is projection/neuron WIDTH or state SIZE (d_mem² is tied to model dim d), since only the latter would motivate a state-expansion module. Three more levers closed this session (all negative-for-recall): memlr in BOTH directions (0.0025 hurt, 0.0075 neutral — no upside either way), mqar_N_power 2.0 (biasing toward high-N HURT high-N recall), mqar_weight 1.0 (val_ppl +13, recall neutral). Detail: neuro_v3_levers_2026_09_09.

Process

Two self-inflicted time sinks, both the same root: hasty kill/relaunch left duplicate training processes that silently collided (cuBLAS contention crash, empty logs), and 4 concurrent 30M screens on one 5080 crashed the same way. Fix banked as a rule: run screens sequentially per GPU, and verify EXACTLY ONE train process after every launch. The WSL $! pid is unreliable — watch via pgrep -f <script>.

57. Capacity screens launched to place the width lever; and a speed investigation finds flagship_v3 is overhead-bound, not FLOP-bound

flagship_v3 throughput is OVERHEAD-bound, not FLOP-bound — which reorders the whole speedup list and rules out FP8. Config arithmetic (B18×512×accum4 = 36,864 tok/step, ~7.4k tok/s → ~5.0 s/step → 15.6 d, matching the config ETA) lands at only ~6–12% MFU on the 5090. Live corroboration: the 90M confirm screen ran at 16% GPU util, throughput falling faster than parameter count predicts — the launch-bound signature. Cause is structural: block/chunk×layers sequential chunk-steps of small kernels + WSL launch overhead + per-chunk activation-checkpoint recompute (gdn2_ckpt_chunks default True) + CUDAGraphs OFF. The clean split: three levers are quality-neutral by construction (need only a throughput probe) — gdn2_ckpt_chunks:false at lower B, gdn2_chunk_size 64→bigger (chunked-WY is EXACT), B/grad_accum rebalance at fixed effective batch; while raising batch_size is NOT free (raises effective batch, needs LR retune; B=18 already near the WDDM cliff). The concrete CUDAGraphs blocker is named: MQAR's variable 2N+1 shape forces a dynamic-compiled 2nd wrapper — bucketing N unblocks it (needs a recall screen). WSD is now re-testable (head_wd fixes its exact divergence mechanism → stoppability). Design doc docs/training_speed_plan_2026_09.md; durable facts neuro_training_speed_plan_2026_09.

Capacity screens launched (executing §56's next step; results not yet in). To place the width lever — does recall keep climbing past ctrlwide's 1.6× toward flagship's 2.4× ratio, and does it reproduce at scale — three screens: screen_nwidth2560_s0_30m (5080, n_neurons 2.5×d) + a guarded overnight seed-1, and a 90M confirm chain screen_nwidth_base640_90m→_wide1024_90m (5090, base 1.0× vs wide 1.6×, MQAR refreshed to the v3 range so N≥32 is measurable). Note ctrlwide already isolated the lever as neuron/projection WIDTH (it raised n_neurons only, d_mem held), and model.py requires d==d_mem for multi-layer so state size can't be raised alone — so the §56 “width vs state-size” question is largely answered as width. Live state + next moves: docs/HANDOFF_2026_09_10b.md.

Process (env): this session drove WSL from the Windows desktop app, whose Git Bash (MSYS) mangles leading-slash paths (a venv python ran as D:/Program Files/Git/..., exit 127) and eats $VARs inside wsl.exe bash -c '...' even in single quotes (a cp silently failed). Fix banked: prefix MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1 MSYS2_ARG_CONV_EXCL="*" and use literal paths / real .sh files, never inline shell vars. Detail: reference_wsl_background_launch (fifth gotcha).

Major version: 1.42 (flagship_v3 is OVERHEAD-bound not FLOP-bound — ~6–12% MFU, 90M screen 16% util → FP8 ruled out; quality-neutral speed levers = ckpt_chunks-off / bigger chunk_size (exact) / B-accum rebalance, batch_size NOT free; MQAR dynamic 2N+1 shape is the CUDAGraphs blocker; WSD re-testable via head_wd. 58. An overnight sweep resolves flagship_v3's two open levers — a +18% free speedup, and “capacity = width, not state”

Throughput: chunk256 + checkpointing-off = +18% tok/s, free. Real-shape 363M probes: gdn2_chunk_size 64→256 (+12.8%, and LEANER 28.5→23.2GB) then gdn2_ckpt_chunks:false (+5% more, 24.8GB) — both quality-neutral (chunked-WY exact; checkpointing is the identity). ~15.6d→~13.2d, frees ~3.7GB. chunk512 REJECTED (max-autotune compile >37min, impractical per launch/resume); batch24 REJECTED (30.3GB WDDM cliff). Applied to configs/flagship_v3_363m.yaml. Detail: neuro_training_speed_plan_2026_09 RESULTS.
MQAR bucketing is recall-equivalent (2 seeds) — CUDAGraphs unblocked. Config-gated mqar_buckets:[4,8,16,32] snaps N to a fixed set (static shape); recall matches uniform [4,32] across 2 seeds. The dynamic MQAR shape was the documented CUDAGraphs blocker, so this clears the recall gate; the speedup itself is not yet measured (next: a 363M cudagraphs+buckets probe).
Capacity: the recall lever is n_neurons WIDTH, not d_mem state. 30M width curve (single-pres N32): 22→41→58→70 as n_neurons goes 1.0→3.5×d, still climbing at 3.5x (2.5x confirmed 2-seed). So the flagship (2.4×d) should WIDEN n_neurons (pending a 90M 2-seed confirm), and the freed ~3.7GB from ckpt-off is exactly the headroom to do it. StateX/state-expansion DEPRIORITIZED (state loses to width), reversing the recall-ceiling doc's tentative bet. Detail: neuro_capacity_width_lever_2026_09_11.

Decisions + process

Glenn's calls: adopt chunk256+ckptoff (done in config); widen n_neurons only after a 90M 2-seed confirm; pursue CUDAGraphs now (probe → commit if it works); KEEP 10B tokens (best-ckpt tracking caps the downside); re-audit before launch; let the scale re-check finish (confirmatory, screen_scale_wide_d1024_6L running at handoff). 59. flagship_v3 launches at 426M — the 90M widen confirmed, VRAM cleared at B=18, CUDAGraphs falls back, and the run goes live at 9,450 tok/s

2026-09-12

The 90M widen-confirm holds — width wins at 3× scale. The two 90M arms from §58 had each been truncated at step 3000 (37M of 90M tok) by a false dstd>3.0 watchdog trip (dstd 4.3/4.0 while loss and val_ppl were still improving); both were resumed to the full 90M budget. At full budget n_neurons 1024 (wide, 1.6×d) beats 640 (base, 1.0×d) on val_ppl (32.91 vs 36.82) and decisively on mid/high-N recall — single-presentation N16/32/64 62/39/23 vs 48/34/11, split-replay N16/32/64 80/62/41 vs 77/61/34 (low-N split is saturated, ties within noise). The §58 30M width-→-recall finding reproduces at ~3× the parameter count.

A compiled B=18 VRAM probe at the new shape peaked at 27.5 GB (nvidia-smi, incl. autotune; 27.3 GB reserved at steady state) — ~2.5 GB under the ~29–30 GB WDDM cliff, essentially the old 363M footprint, so B=18 held with no step-down and Glenn's “never below B=18” guard never fired. The freed headroom from §58's ckpt_chunks-off is what made the widen fit.

CUDAGraphs still won't run — bucketing did not unblock it. §58 hoped mqar_buckets (static MQAR shape) would clear the CUDAGraphs blocker. A 363M max-autotune+buckets probe crashed with the exact documented failure — RuntimeError: accessing tensor output of CUDAGraphs that has been overwritten at model.py:96 (the RMSNorm forward), identical to neuro_cudagraphs_incompat_2026_08_09. The shared-weight MQAR wrapper overwrites the captured RMSNorm output regardless of bucketing; the real fix needs an MQAR restructure, out of scope for a launch. Kept max-autotune-no-cudagraphs — which the VRAM probe had already clocked at ~9,450 tok/s regardless.
flagship_v3 is LIVE. Launched detached (setsid) on the 5090 and confirmed training at step 1000 — 9,450 tok/s, loss 13.0 descending, VRAM 27.3 GB steady, GPU 73%. At 9,450 tok/s the 10B-token ceiling is ~13–14 days of wall-clock — not the 20–30 d the stale ~3,800 tok/s figure implied — and best-checkpoint tracking caps the real downside well before 10B.

The arch (flagship_v3, 426M)

A 9-layer spiking LM: d=1280, n_neurons=3840 (3.0×d), d_mem=1280. Eight GDN-2 chunked-dense spiking blocks (delta_rule + decay_gate, beta_floor:0.1, gdn2_chunk_size:256, gdn2_ckpt_chunks:false) plus one attention layer at position 4 (10 heads, attention_qk_norm). engram_v2 factual memory (K=8192, heads=4, orders [2,3], rank 64); MTP heads (offset 2, weight 0.3, depth 2). Trained on MQAR-range [4,32] weight 0.5, sparsity λ=0.5 target 0.15, with NorMuon+AdamW (muon_lr 0.005, muon_fix_gate_matrices, AdamW lr 5e-4) and head_weight_decay:1.0. block 512, B=18 × grad_accum 4 (36,864 tok/step), max_tokens 10B, compile_mode: max-autotune-no-cudagraphs.

Decisions + process

Glenn's pre-sleep calls: widen to 3.0×d with a B=18 VRAM fallback (fell back to the largest safe width only if the cliff was busted — it wasn't); try CUDAGraphs only if ≥12% faster within a time-box (it crashed → clean fallback); keep 10B tokens; launch autonomously only if every guardrail passed; record to journey + to-do only on a confirmed launch. All guardrails passed (win confirmed, VRAM fits, audit clean, fresh-run). A config-gated watchdog_dstd_thresh (default 3.0, byte-identical when unset) was added after the dstd>3.0 wire false-truncated both earlier 90M runs; set to 6.0 on the 90M configs and the flagship, with the val_ppl tripwire left as the real divergence guard. Process lesson banked: with log_every:1000 at ~9.4k tok/s the first log line only lands ~50–60 min after launch, so a 50-min “no step” watcher false-alarmed a healthy compile-then-train as a stall — the fluctuating GPU util + live inductor compile workers were the tell that it was working, not hung.

60. Post-training gets a plan and its first probe — the tokenizer is Llama-3 (a teacher IS usable), ORPO holds where DPO collapsed, and the 5080 is too small for 363M post-training

2026-09-14

With flagship_v3 cooking healthily on the 5090 (val_ppl descending past 35 at step ~52k), we turned to the post-training question: how to make the flagship optimal on every axis we can still influence after pretraining. A literature sweep across 2025–2026 (DeepSeek, Qwen, Kimi) reset the plan — details in neuro_posttrain_v3_prep_2026_09_13. Headlines: on-policy distillation is the 2026 workhorse for small models (openPangu Embedded-1B reaches Qwen3-1.7B parity at 1B via distillation-by-student; DeepSeek-V4 consolidates with it); reasoning has “diminishing value” below 1B (MobileLLM-R1), so at 426M we target instruction-following + the moat, not reasoning benchmarks; and our DPO collapse (§52) is textbook likelihood displacement — the chosen-response probability falls and mass shifts to opposite-meaning tokens.

The tokenizer is Llama-3 — a teacher IS usable for v3, and the logit-projection infra already exists. Correcting a wrong assumption from the research pass: src/tokenizer.py is meta-llama/Meta-Llama-3-8B (128,256 vocab); train.bin was tokenized with it and projected to our reduced vocab via data/vocab_map_32768.npy (flagship_v3 vocab 32768; the old flagship base is also 32768, so 363M SFT configs are arch-faithful). src/kd.py already does verified teacher→student logit projection (top-128, 128k→32k, gather-based) and train_sft.py builds the inverse student→Llama map. Teachers are cached locally (Llama-3.1-8B, 3.2-1B). So logit-level distillation is NOT blocked by our tokenizer — only on-policy distillation needs a rollout+inverse-map build on top.
ORPO holds coherence where DPO catastrophically collapsed. Glenn picked the ORPO/SPO safety probe as the thing to build. We added a reference-free objective: orpo to src/train_dpo.py (SFT-NLL on chosen + λ odds-ratio, with a stable log1mexp); a CPU test (temp/test_orpo_loss.py) confirmed the loss is finite and its gradient pulls the chosen log-prob UP — the exact anti-likelihood-displacement property DPO lacks. The probe (init from sft_flagship_363m/step_0003663, the same UltraFeedback pairs DPO collapsed on, λ=0.1) stayed coherent and factually correct (“The capital of France is Paris.”) from step 200 through step 675 (11.1M tok) — vs DPO's incoherent word-soup by step 100 (~0.8M tok), a 12×+ margin. A single step-500 greedy wobble recovered by step 600, a reminder that greedy gen-checks are noisy (the §52 lesson).
The 5080 is too small for 363M post-training — the probe OOM'd on a WDDM spill and never got its recall check. At batch_size 4 (8 concatenated chosen+rejected seqs) eager, the 16GB 5080 spilled into WDDM shared RAM (torch reserved 20.1 GB > 16 GB physical), degrading 306→200 tok/s, then OOM'd at step 675 — a VRAM failure, not a quality one. The auto recall_eval --replay split was written at --B 128 (a 32GB-5090 value) and would also OOM; it was killed at start, so the memory-pathway half of the ORPO win is still UNMEASURED (orpo_probe_363m/step_0000500.pt, 8.2M tok, saved for it). And at ~300 tok/s eager, the staged A curriculum-vs-flat SFT chain is infeasible in a one-night 5080 window — the earlier ~1800 tok/s estimate was 3–6× too optimistic. Sizing rules banked in neuro_posttrain_v3_prep_2026_09_13.

Decisions + process

Glenn's calls: build only the ORPO probe this window (on-policy KD and GRPO deferred); then, mid-run, “don't restart on the 5080 now” — so the recall check and the A chain are parked until a suitable GPU/window is free. Housekeeping the same session: MEMORY.md consolidated 20.5→11.0 KB into a themed pointer index (details stay in per-topic files, read on demand). The 5090 flagship was untouched throughout. Staged-but-held: configs/orpo_probe_363m.yaml, configs/sft_curric_*_363m.yaml (need batch_size 8→≤4 before any 5080 run), temp/run_orpo_probe.sh, temp/run_ad_chain.sh, watchdogs temp/v3_watchdog.sh + temp/sweep_watchdog.sh.

Major version: 1.45 (post-training planned + first probe. Tokenizer is Llama-3 -> a teacher IS usable for v3 (kd.py logit-projection verified, teachers cached); logit-KD not blocked, only on-policy needs a build. ORPO built in train_dpo.py (objective key, reference-free, CPU-verified) HOLDS coherence to step 675/11.1M tok where DPO word-souped by step 100/0.8M -- 12x+ margin -- but OOM'd on a 5080 WDDM spill (reserved 20.1GB>16GB) and its recall check is unmeasured (step_0000500 saved). 5080 too small/slow for 363M post-training (~300 tok/s, batch<=4, recall --B<=32); A chain needs the 5090 or days. MEMORY.md consolidated 20.5->11KB. See neuro_posttrain_v3_prep_2026_09_13 + HANDOFF_2026_09_14.)

61. ORPO's recall half comes back a pass, the 5080 sweep is rebuilt to survive being paused, and the architecture gets its first marketing language

2026-09-15

Chapter 60 left one thing unmeasured: whether ORPO, which held coherence 12×+ past DPO's collapse, also preserved the Hebbian memory pathway — the recall_eval had been killed by the 5080 OOM before it ran. With the 5080 free this session, we ran it against the saved orpo_probe_363m/step_0000500.pt: recall_eval --replay split --runs sft_flagship_363m,orpo_probe_363m --B 32.

ORPO preserves recall — it is the safe preference stage for v3. Among-candidates accuracy matches the SFT base within noise (100/100/78/44/16% vs 100/100/81/44/19% at N=4/8/16/32/64) and mean target-rank is actually BETTER at every N≥16 (443/3804/8647 vs 830/4771/10725). Coherence (§60) and recall (here) both clear, so ORPO is banked as v3's preference method; DPO stays dead.

Glenn flagged that the first run looked suspiciously fast (~a minute, the 5080 barely lighting up) — a fair challenge. But it is an eval of two existing checkpoints, not a training run, so a short runtime is expected. We reran it under /usr/bin/time -v with live GPU sampling as proof: 37.5s wall-clock, 14.4 GB peak on the 5080, byte-identical numbers on the repeat (fixed-seed eval). The result is real.

The rest of the session turned the parked A-curriculum sweep into something that can run alongside the flagship across days rather than dying at dawn. The one-night run_ad_chain.sh was replaced by a resumable temp/run_5080_chain.sh: no global deadline, per-stage .done markers, and it stops on any non-zero exit so a relaunch re-enters the same stage. We confirmed train_sft.py auto-resumes from the latest checkpoint (model + optimizer states + step + tokens, src/train_sft.py:215-226), so pause = kill, resume = relaunch. Configs moved to batch_size 4 / grad_accum 8 (batch 8 is exactly what spilled the ORPO probe) and ckpt_every 250 (~50 min, so a pause loses <1h). A first deadline-bounded night got A0 (flat baseline control) to step 3000 (~49M/70M tok) before the timeout cut it; a partial-A0 eval gave arc_easy 35.8%. Throughput (~1,300 tok/s compiled) confirms §60's sizing verdict: the full sweep wants days, not one night.

Finally, the first outward-facing framing of the architecture: two marketing blurbs (H:\Projects\PsycorAI - Backup\blogs\marketing_blurbs.html), a technical one for industry/investors and a plain-English one, covering spiking sparsity (a ~85% inference-energy claim — needs a measured source before it reaches investors), online Hebbian fast-weight memory, the self-refine loop, the ~34% ppl win over a size-matched Transformer, and — at Glenn's steer, given alignment is the hottest topic in AI — native interpretability: tracing the neuronal origin and propagation path of an output. That last claim is phrased as architecture-enabled, not yet demonstrated; a to-do now tracks researching + building + testing origin/path tracing (future blog material), alongside a TTFT / tok-s benchmark vs the Transformer.

Major version: 1.46 (ORPO's recall half MEASURED = PASS: matches SFT base within noise on among-cand [100/100/78/44/16 vs 100/100/81/44/19 @ N=4/8/16/32/64], BETTER mean-rank every N≥16 [443/3804/8647 vs 830/4771/10725] -> ORPO is v3's safe preference stage, coherence+recall both clear; reran under time -v [37.5s/14.4GB, reproduced] to answer a skepticism check. Curriculum A-sweep made RESUMABLE: temp/run_5080_chain.sh [no deadline, .done markers, stops-on-nonzero], train_sft auto-resumes [model+opt+step], configs batch4/ga8/ckpt250; A0 partial step3000 ~49M/70M, arc_easy 35.8%; ~1300 tok/s confirms sweep needs days. Marketing blurbs drafted [technical+plain]: spiking ~85% energy, Hebbian memory, self-refine, ~34% ppl win, native origin/path interpretability [architecture-enabled, undemonstrated]. To-dos: TTFT/tok-s vs Transformer + origin/path-tracing research. CLAUDE.md: answer concisely. See neuro_posttrain_v3_prep_2026_09_13 + HANDOFF_2026_09_15.)

62. A false watchdog trip on a healthy flagship, an EMA fix that vindicates it, and the curriculum sweep's verdict — recall yes, downstream a wash

2026-09-16 – 09-20

Five days of flagship_v3 (§59) running alongside the parked 5080 work (§61) — during which the run's own divergence guard turned on it.

The val_ppl tripwire killed a perfectly healthy flagship at step 106k. val_ppl set a lucky-low 27.61 @100k, then two ordinary noise draws (31.31, 30.06) each cleared best*1.02 — two consecutive, so the §52 tripwire saved a ckpt and sys.exit(2). But every structural signal said healthy: the LM-head norm was falling (77.7→66, head_weight_decay:1.0 working — the exact opposite of the §51 v1 runaway), β 0% dead on every block, memory-Δ stable ~+380, and the per-step train-loss sat in its normal noisy 4–7 band (it had hit 7.4 at step 75k and recovered). The same run had already spiked 30.67→32.27→28.62 once and recovered to new bests. It was noise.

Root cause: the tripwire compared against the best single eval with a tight 2% band and patience 2 — so one lucky-low eval permanently lowers the bar and the run's own ±2–3 ppl noise trips it. The fix keeps the guard but makes it noise-robust: optional EMA smoothing added to _valppl_watchdog_step (watchdog_valppl_ema_alpha, default 0.0 = old raw behavior, so every other run and test is byte-identical), with the flagship set to ema_alpha 0.4 / tol 0.07 / patience 2. A 15-case unit test (temp/test_valppl_watchdog.py) confirms the real 106k series no longer trips while the v1 head-norm divergence and a synthetic runaway still do; the session notify-watchdog threshold was bumped 1.02→1.07 to match.

The resume vindicated the call. Restarted from step_0106000.pt (the model was healthy there — no progress discarded), val_ppl kept right on descending — to ~25 by step 138k, well below the 27.61 that had "diverged" — and the new EMA wire absorbed a later raw 26.72 bounce (ema +1.6%) without tripping. The false-trip was noise; the retune holds. Durable facts: neuro_flagship_v3_valppl_watchdog_retune.

Two smaller env notes: a one-off 5080 bus dropout (nvidia-smi: "Unable to determine the device handle... Unknown Error", GPU1 gone) cleared on a normal reboot with the 5090 flagship untouched (reference_5080_bus_error_reboot); and a WSL nvidia-smi quirk once mis-attributed a running video game's 5 GB on the 5080 to the flagship PID — nearly read as a config bug, but it was the game.

The curriculum sweep's verdict

The resumable A-sweep (§61) finished across two free-5080 nights: A0 flat baseline / A1 curriculum-reasoning / A2 fast-init-from-A1 / D evals, token-matched at 363M.

Curriculum's one real win is recall. A1 (reasoning stage) beats flat baseline at every mid/high N on among-candidates accuracy — N16/32/64 91/47/25 vs base 84/41/19 (A2 91/44/16, giving back high-N). The reasoning curriculum measurably strengthens the memory pathway.
Downstream is a wash. The chain's blanket timeout 25m had killed hellaswag/arc_challenge mid-run, so a dedicated re-run (temp/run_5080_evals.sh) with right-sized timeouts firmed it up: all three endpoints sit at chance on hellaswag (~28%, N=6000), arc_challenge (~19–21%, N=299), and mmlu (~27%, N=192). Only arc_easy is above chance (base 36.0 / reason 32.8 / fast 38.1) and its spread is near noise. A 426M-class base just isn't above chance on these, so curriculum choice doesn't move benchmarks at this scale — decide curriculum on recall, not downstream. Sizing banked: lm_eval is 0.87 s/item + ~44 s load on the 5080 (reference_lm_eval_timeouts) — size per-task, never blanket. Details: neuro_curriculum_sweep_outcome.

Post-training is now recipe-complete, gated only on the base. SFT works (§60), ORPO is the chosen preference stage (§60/§61), the KD teacher is verified usable (§60), and curriculum is tested (recall-only). Nothing left to build — just let flagship_v3 finish (~62% at step 167k, ~Sep 24–25) and then run SFT → ORPO on the finished base.

Major version: 1.47 (flagship_v3's val_ppl tripwire FALSE-tripped a healthy run @106k -- lucky-low best 27.61 then noise 31.31/30.06 cleared best*1.02 x2 -> exit2; diagnosed noise-not-divergence [head norm FALLING, β 0% dead, memory-Δ stable, loss normal band, prior spike-recover].
